---
description: Move a project written against flutter3d 0.8 to 1.0.0-rc.1, with one command for the dependencies, dart fix for the renames and moves, and a list of what is left for you.
---

# Migrating from 0.8 to 1.0

1.0 is the release the engine promises to keep: from it on, a break comes
only in a major version. To get there, 0.8's API changed in 1566 places,
344 of them left to you; 380 are backend internals no application called.
The rest a program carries out for you, and three
tools share the work. All three read one table,
`flutter3d_build/lib/migrations/0.8_to_1.0.yaml`, so they agree about what
changed.

<div class="goal">
<ul>
<li>Your dependencies on 1.0.0-rc.1, your imports where the names now live</li>
<li>Renames, moved names and deprecated calls rewritten by tools</li>
<li>A short list of what needs a decision from you, each with a TODO in the code</li>
</ul>
</div>

## Run the migration

Commit or stash first: the migration edits your files in place, and a clean
`git diff` is the easiest way to read what it did.

```bash
dart pub global activate flutter3d_build 1.0.0-rc.1
dart pub global activate flutter3d_lints 1.0.0-rc.1
dart pub global run flutter3d_build:migrate --dry-run .   # see what it would do
dart pub global run flutter3d_build:migrate .
```

Activate the tools globally rather than adding them to your project. Your
pubspec still asks for 0.8, and a 1.0 tool cannot resolve beside it until the
migration has moved the constraints.

`migrate` works through the project in this order:

1. **Dependencies.** Every dependency on a flutter3d package moves to
   `^1.0.0-rc.1`, or to the package's own number for the few that are
   versioned apart (`pad_input`, `pointer_lock`, `flame_multiplayer`). Path
   and git dependencies are left alone and listed.
2. **Imports** of libraries that moved to another package are rewritten.
3. `flutter pub get` (or `dart pub get` for a plain Dart package).
4. **`dart fix --apply`.** Each flutter3d package ships a `lib/fix_data.yaml`
   generated from the table. It renames what was renamed and imports moved
   names from where they live now. These are the same fixes your editor
   offers one at a time.
5. **Rewrites that need the resolved code**, which `dart fix` cannot express:
   the deprecated capability getters (`device.supportsWireframe` becomes
   `device.features.has(DeviceFeature.wireframe)`), and `implements X` for
   the types 1.0 made `base mixin class`es. This step is
   `dart pub global run flutter3d_lints:migrate`, and `migrate` runs it for
   you.
6. **Dependencies again**, for the packages the new imports need, such as
   `flutter3d_core` when a name left the `flutter3d` facade.
7. **The report.** It lists the files changed and what is left for you.
   Every item on the list also has a `// TODO(flutter3d-1.0):` above it in
   the code, with a link to its line below.

`--from 0.8` is the default and the only source version for now.
`--dry-run` makes the same changes in a copy next to your project, prints
the report and removes the copy.

Afterwards, run `flutter analyze`. What is left is either on the report or
is a change in behaviour the analyzer cannot see. The table below says which
entries change behaviour.

## In the editor

`flutter3d_lints` is also an analysis server plugin. Listed in
`analysis_options.yaml`, it reports each use of something 1.0 changed: a
`flutter3d_migrate` warning with a quick fix where the change can be
written, and `flutter3d_migrate_by_hand` with the guide's link where it
cannot.

```yaml
plugins:
  flutter3d_lints: ^1.0.0-rc.1
```

The plugin also turns on the fixed-step rules described in its README. A
plugin's quick fixes are applied one at a time; `dart fix` does not apply
them in bulk, which is why step 5 above exists.

## GameLoop to EngineLoop

<a id="gameloop-to-engineloop"></a>`GameLoop` still works in 1.0 and is
deprecated until 2.0. `EngineLoop` replaces it: the fixed step runs as named
systems in ordered phases, and plugins add theirs to the same loop. The
change is a shape, not a rename, so no tool makes it for you:

```dart
// 0.8
final loop = GameLoop(input: input, onStep: (dt) => world.step(dt));
// every frame:
loop.advance(dt);

// 1.0
final loop = EngineLoop(input: input)
  ..addSystem('world', LoopPhase.physics, (ctx) => world.step(ctx.dt));
// every frame:
loop.frame(dt);
```

`paused`, `alpha`, `playback`, `recorders`, `drainLook` and `longestFrame`
keep their names. `EngineLoop` adds `world:` (a `WorldTiming`, the step rate
and time scale) and `catchUp:` (what to do when a frame falls behind).
Their defaults are what `GameLoop` did. In a Flame game, `HasFixedStep` runs
an `EngineLoop` already: read `engineLoop`, and register your step work in
`installStepSystems`.

## Every change, by package

Each entry links here from its diagnostic and its TODO. How each one is
carried out:

- **`dart fix`**: applied by `migrate` (step 4) and offered in your editor.
- **`migrate`**: applied by `migrate` (steps 2 and 5).
- **by hand**: on the report, with a TODO in your code.
- **nothing to do**: the classifier counts it as a break, but no call you
  can write is affected. It is listed so you know it was checked.

<!-- migration-table:start -->

*Generated from `flutter3d_build/lib/migrations/` — 1566 entries from 0.8.5 to 1.0.0-rc.1, 344 by hand: 97 `migrate`, 344 by hand, 289 nothing to do, 456 `dart fix`, 380 gone internal.*

### `flame_flutter3d`

- <a id="flame_flutter3d-ActorSystemComponent"></a>**`ActorSystemComponent`** (by hand). The `step:` parameter (a `FixedStep`) is now `timing:` (a `WorldTiming`) and `catchUp:` (a `CatchUp`), used only when the game does not step the component itself; the public `step` field is gone.
- <a id="flame_flutter3d-BillboardAtlas"></a>**`BillboardAtlas`** (nothing to do). Only the engine's material type in its signature changed: `Material` is `RenderMaterial` (`core-Material-RenderMaterial` renames it).
- <a id="flame_flutter3d-BridgeSpace"></a>**`BridgeSpace`** (`migrate`). `BridgeSpace` is a base mixin class: `implements BridgeSpace` becomes `with BridgeSpace`, on a `base` class.
- <a id="rc-flame-Bridged3d-tint"></a>**`Bridged3d`** (by hand). See `Drawn3d`: `tint` is a settable `LinearColor`.
- <a id="4a-flame_flutter3d-CameraSyncController-takeRest"></a>**`CameraSyncController.takeRest`** (`dart fix`). `CameraSyncController.takeRest` is `captureRest`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="flame_flutter3d-CellGridComponent"></a>**`CellGridComponent`** (nothing to do). Only the engine's material type in its signature changed: `Material` is `RenderMaterial` (`core-Material-RenderMaterial` renames it).
- <a id="rc-flame-CellGridComponent-color"></a>**`CellGridComponent`** (by hand). `CellGridComponent(color:)` is a vertex colour, a linear `LinearColor`: `Vector4(r, g, b, a)` is `LinearColor(r, g, b, a)`.
- <a id="4a-flame_flutter3d-CellGridComponent-CellGridComponent"></a>**`CellGridComponent.CellGridComponent`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-flame_flutter3d-CellGridComponent-centreOf"></a>**`CellGridComponent.centreOf`** (`dart fix`). `CellGridComponent.centreOf` is `centerOf`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-flame_flutter3d-CellGridComponent-colour"></a>**`CellGridComponent.colour`** (`dart fix`). `CellGridComponent.colour` is `color`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="flame_flutter3d-ChaseCamera"></a>**`ChaseCamera`** (`dart fix`). `ChaseCamera` is `FlameChaseCamera`. The racing genre has a `ChaseCamera` of its own, so the Flame bridge's is `FlameChaseCamera`, with the same constructor and members.
- <a id="flame_flutter3d-ChaseCameraComponent"></a>**`ChaseCameraComponent`** (`dart fix`). `ChaseCameraComponent` is `FlameChaseCameraComponent`. Renamed with `FlameChaseCamera`, which it carries; the same constructor and members.
- <a id="rc-flame-Drawn3d-tint"></a>**`Drawn3d`** (by hand). `Drawn3d.tint` is a settable `LinearColor` where it was a `Vector4` changed in place, so a `TintEffect` assigns it. A class implementing `Drawn3d` declares `LinearColor tint = LinearColor.white;` (a field, or a getter and a setter) in place of `Vector4 get tint`.
- <a id="f4-flame-FlameInputBridge"></a>**`FlameInputBridge`** (by hand). `FlameInputBridge` takes `actions:`, an `ActionMap`, where it took `bindings:`: the map a native game's `DesktopInput` reads. Wrap an old table as `ActionMap(actions: ActionSet.common, buttons: table)`.
- <a id="rc-flame-Flutter3dFlameWidget-clearColor"></a>**`Flutter3dFlameWidget`** (by hand). `Flutter3dFlameWidget(clearColor:)` takes a `LinearColor`, encoded to sRGB for the view once a frame. The `Vector4` it took was sRGB, so the same picture is `clearColor: LinearColor.fromSrgb(r, g, b)`.
- <a id="flame_flutter3d-HasFixedStep"></a>**`HasFixedStep`** (by hand). Only code that implements `HasFixedStep` has anything to do: it now has to provide `createEngineLoop`, `engineLoop`, `installStepSystems`. Code that calls a `HasFixedStep` needs no change.
- <a id="flame-HasFixedStep-fixedStep"></a>**`HasFixedStep.fixedStep`** (by hand). `fixedStep` is gone: the mixin runs an `EngineLoop`. Read `engineLoop`, override `createEngineLoop` to give it your own `WorldTiming` and `CatchUp`, and register step work in `installStepSystems`.
- <a id="rc-flame-HasFlutter3d-clearColor"></a>**`HasFlutter3d`** (by hand). `HasFlutter3d.clearColor` is a `LinearColor`, linear, assigned whole, where it was a `Vector4` of sRGB numbers changed in place. The numbers it held keep the sky: `clearColor.setValues(0.55, 0.75, 0.95, 1)` is `clearColor = LinearColor.fromSrgb(0.55, 0.75, 0.95)`; a read of `.z` is `.b`, in linear light. An `AtmosphereComponent` assigns the day's sky to it, as it wrote into it before.
- <a id="rc-flame-InstancedObject3dComponent-colours"></a>**`InstancedObject3dComponent`** (by hand). `color` (the constructor's and the field) and `tint` are linear `LinearColor`s; `tint` is assigned whole. `color: Vector4(r, g, b, a)` is `color: LinearColor(r, g, b, a)`, the same numbers.
- <a id="rc-flame-Node3dComponent-tint"></a>**`Node3dComponent`** (by hand). `tint` is a linear `LinearColor` assigned whole: `component.tint.setValues(1, 0.3, 0.25, 1)` is `component.tint = const LinearColor(1, 0.3, 0.25)`, and `tint.x`, `.y`, `.z`, `.w` are `tint.r`, `.g`, `.b`, `.a`. The numbers are the same; the tint was linear already.
- <a id="rc-flame-Object3dComponent-tint"></a>**`Object3dComponent`** (by hand). `tint` is a linear `LinearColor` assigned whole; see `rc-flame-Node3dComponent-tint`.
- <a id="flame_flutter3d-PhysicsStepComponent"></a>**`PhysicsStepComponent`** (by hand). `dynamics` is typed as the `RigidDynamics` interface now, which the Dart solver `Dynamics` and the native one both implement. Declare what you read it into as `RigidDynamics`; a call that needs the Dart solver's own members casts with `as Dynamics`.
- <a id="flame_flutter3d-PhysicsStepComponent-step"></a>**`PhysicsStepComponent`** (by hand). The `step:` parameter (a `FixedStep`) is now `timing:` (a `WorldTiming`) and `catchUp:` (a `CatchUp`), used only when the game does not step the component itself; the public `step` field is gone.
- <a id="flame_flutter3d-RigidBodyComponent"></a>**`RigidBodyComponent`** (by hand). `removeFrom` is a `RigidDynamics?` now: pass any solver, and declare a variable you read it into as `RigidDynamics?`.
- <a id="flame_flutter3d-TiledWorld3d"></a>**`TiledWorld3d`** (nothing to do). Only the engine's material type in its signature changed: `Material` is `RenderMaterial` (`core-Material-RenderMaterial` renames it).
- <a id="rc-flame-TintEffect"></a>**`TintEffect.TintEffect`** (by hand). `TintEffect(color, controller)` takes a linear `LinearColor`: `TintEffect(Vector4(1, 0, 0, 1), c)` is `TintEffect(const LinearColor(1, 0, 0), c)`.
- <a id="rc-flame-TrailComponent-color"></a>**`TrailComponent`** (by hand). `TrailComponent(color:)` and `color` are a linear `LinearColor`, white by default: `Vector4(r, g, b, a)` is `LinearColor(r, g, b, a)`.
- <a id="4a-flame_flutter3d-TrailComponent-TrailComponent"></a>**`TrailComponent.TrailComponent`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-flame_flutter3d-TrailComponent-color"></a>**`TrailComponent.color`** (nothing to do). `color` is `colour` under its American name, which the rename entry for `TrailComponent.colour` carries; an implementer overrides it by the new name.
- <a id="4a-flame_flutter3d-TrailComponent-colour"></a>**`TrailComponent.colour`** (`dart fix`). `TrailComponent.colour` is `color`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="f4-flame-ViewCamera-framing"></a>**`ViewCamera`** (nothing to do). `rig`, `stiffness` and `view` are getters over the camera's `ViewFraming` and `VirtualCamera` rather than fields; reading them is unchanged, and nothing outside the class could assign them.

### `flame_flutter3d_audio`

- <a id="flame_audio-AudioSceneComponent"></a>**`AudioSceneComponent`** (nothing to do). `play` returns the emitter under its 1.0 name, `AudioEmitter`, the class `SoundEmitter` became; `audio_core-SoundEmitter` renames every `SoundEmitter` a caller wrote, so what it assigns the result to still fits.
- <a id="f4-audio-AudioSceneComponent-close"></a>**`AudioSceneComponent.close`** (`dart fix`). `AudioSceneComponent.close` is `dispose`. `close` is `dispose`, the one teardown verb. It stops every sound and disposes the speakers, as `close` did, and an `open` after it does nothing: a game that wants its sound again adds a new `AudioSceneComponent`.
- <a id="f4-audio-OpenedSpeakers"></a>**`OpenedSpeakers`** (by hand). The `OpenedSpeakers` record is gone: `AudioSceneComponent.opener` is a `Future<Speakers> Function()`. An opener that made the record `(scene: scene, close: close)` returns `Speakers(backend: backend, scene: scene)`, and one that answered null for no device throws `AudioDeviceException`.
- <a id="flame_audio-SoundEmitterComponent"></a>**`SoundEmitterComponent`** (nothing to do). `emitter` is typed as `AudioEmitter`, the class `SoundEmitter` became; `audio_core-SoundEmitter` renames every `SoundEmitter` a caller wrote.
- <a id="4a-flame_audio-SoundEmitterComponent-SoundEmitterComponent"></a>**`SoundEmitterComponent.SoundEmitterComponent`** (`dart fix`). A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-flame_audio-SoundEmitterComponent-playing"></a>**`SoundEmitterComponent.playing`** (`dart fix`). `SoundEmitterComponent.playing` is `isPlaying`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.

### `flame_multiplayer`

- <a id="flame_multiplayer-BatonStream"></a>**`BatonStream`** (nothing to do). The constructor's `holding:` was declared as `this._holding`, a private named parameter, and is a plain `bool holding` now. A call already passed `holding:`, so no call changes; only the signature's spelling did.
- <a id="4a-flame_multiplayer-BatonStream-holding"></a>**`BatonStream.holding`** (`dart fix`). `BatonStream.holding` is `isHolding`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-flame_multiplayer-BatonStream-take"></a>**`BatonStream.take`** (`dart fix`). `BatonStream.take` is `claim`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="f1-net-LoopbackParty-moved"></a>**`LoopbackParty`** (nothing to do). Declared by `flutter3d_net` now and re-exported here, as `PeerWire`.
- <a id="f1-net-LoopbackWire-moved"></a>**`LoopbackWire`** (nothing to do). Declared by `flutter3d_net` now and re-exported here, as `PeerWire`.
- <a id="f1-net-PeerRoom-moved"></a>**`PeerRoom`** (nothing to do). Declared by `flutter3d_net` now and re-exported here, as `PeerWire`.
- <a id="4a-flame_multiplayer-PeerRoom-met"></a>**`PeerRoom.met`** (`dart fix`). `PeerRoom.met` is `hasMet`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="flame_multiplayer-PeerWire"></a>**`PeerWire`** (by hand). `PeerWire` is an `abstract base class`: an adapter `extends PeerWire` and keeps its `send` and `listen`. `slot`, `state`, `listenFrom` and `close` have defaults; override `close` and `state` for a connection that has them.
- <a id="f1-net-PeerWire-moved"></a>**`PeerWire`** (nothing to do). `flutter3d_net` declares it now, on the 1.0 line; `flame_multiplayer` re-exports it, so an import of either compiles.
- <a id="f1-net-PeerWire-const"></a>**`PeerWire.PeerWire`** (nothing to do). The constructor is no longer `const`: a wire holds its listeners. An adapter's own constructor was not `const` either, since a connection is not a constant.
- <a id="f1-net-PeerWire-listen"></a>**`PeerWire.listen`** (by hand). A wire has several listeners now: `listen` and `listenFrom` are the base class's, add a listener and return the `Registration` that takes it away. An adapter no longer overrides them; it calls `deliver(message)` (a party's wire `deliver(message, from: slot)`) with each message that arrives, and drops the field that held the one listener. A wire wrapping another overrides `listenFrom` to hand back the inner wire's registration.
- <a id="flame_multiplayer-RollbackPlay"></a>**`RollbackPlay`** (by hand). Use `RollbackSession` with `localSlot`, `inputDelay: 3` and `maxRollbackFrames: 20` (`RollbackPlay`'s defaults); `captureLocalFrame` is `capture`, `applyAndStep` reads `frames[0]`/`frames[1]` where it read `bySlot`, and `endsAt`, `agreedEnd` and `connected` are on the session.
- <a id="flame_multiplayer-RollbackSession"></a>**`RollbackSession`** (by hand). `RollbackSession` is the one rollback, for two to thirty-two machines: it takes `localSlot` (the wire's `slot` by default) and `players`, its `applyAndStep` receives every slot's frame by slot, and `onSettled` also receives the frames.
- <a id="f1-net-RollbackSession-moved"></a>**`RollbackSession`** (nothing to do). Declared by `flutter3d_net` now and re-exported here, as `PeerWire`.
- <a id="multiplayer-RollbackSession-early"></a>**`RollbackSession`** (nothing to do). A frame more than `maxStepsAhead` (600) steps ahead of the session is dropped and counted in `droppedEarly` rather than held without bound, and a frame that arrives from a room maps its sender to the session's own slot, so two peers of a room no longer drop each other's input.
- <a id="f1-net-WireHello-moved"></a>**`WireHello`** (nothing to do). Declared by `flutter3d_net` now and re-exported here, as `PeerWire`.
- <a id="f1-net-protocol-3"></a>**`WireHello.currentProtocolMajor`** (nothing to do). Protocol 3: a rollback's frames carry the reserved key `"f3d": "rollback"` (`PeerWire.engineKey`). A machine on protocol 2 is refused in the hello with the version to update to, and a game's own message never sets `f3d`.
- <a id="f1-net-WireState-moved"></a>**`WireState`** (nothing to do). Declared by `flutter3d_net` now and re-exported here, as `PeerWire`.

### `flutter3d`

- <a id="internal-flutter3d"></a>**1 name was the package's own** (gone internal): they are not exported since 1.0.0-rc.1, and the import of `flutter3d` that brought them gets one diagnostic for all of them. <a id="flutter3d-package-flutter3d-parity_scene-dart"></a>`package:flutter3d/parity_scene.dart`. A backend's own tests import it from `package:flutter3d_core/src/engine/render/parity_scene.dart`.
- <a id="flutter3d-BundleAssetSource-missing"></a>**`BundleAssetSource`** (by hand). A key the asset bundle does not hold throws `AssetNotFoundException` (a `Flutter3dException`) naming the key, where it threw the platform's `FlutterError`. Catch `AssetNotFoundException`, or the root, instead.
- <a id="flutter3d-MaterialDecoder"></a>**`MaterialDecoder`** (by hand). An `abstract base class` since 1.0: write `extends MaterialDecoder` where you wrote `implements MaterialDecoder`, and mark the class `final`, `base` or `sealed`.
- <a id="flutter3d-ModelAsset"></a>**`ModelAsset`** (nothing to do). Only the engine's material type in its signature changed: `Material` is `RenderMaterial` (`core-Material-RenderMaterial` renames it).
- <a id="f2-flutter3d-ModelAsset-release"></a>**`ModelAsset.release`** (`migrate`). `ModelAsset.release` becomes `x.dispose()`. `dispose()` is the one verb for ending what holds GPU memory. The asset remembers the device it was uploaded to, so the call takes nothing; a second call does nothing.
- <a id="flutter3d-ModelInstance"></a>**`ModelInstance`** (nothing to do). The constructor's private `_drawn` became the public named `drawn`, and a private `_slots` was added. Nothing outside the library could pass either, so every call still compiles.
- <a id="flutter3d-assetDeviceClass"></a>**`assetDeviceClass`** (by hand). There is no process-wide device class since 1.0: pass `deviceClass:` to `loadModelAsset` or `loadModelByPath`, and set `HotSwap.instance.deviceClass` for hot reload.
- <a id="flutter3d-bindMaterial"></a>**`bindMaterial`** (nothing to do). Only the engine's material type in its signature changed: `Material` is `RenderMaterial` (`core-Material-RenderMaterial` renames it).
- <a id="flutter3d-bindSurfaceMaterial"></a>**`bindSurfaceMaterial`** (nothing to do). Only the engine's material type in its signature changed: `Material` is `RenderMaterial` (`core-Material-RenderMaterial` renames it).
- <a id="flutter3d-loadMaterial"></a>**`loadMaterial`** (nothing to do). Only the engine's material type in its signature changed: `Material` is `RenderMaterial` (`core-Material-RenderMaterial` renames it).
- <a id="flutter3d-loadModelAsset-missing"></a>**`loadModelAsset`** (by hand). A release build missing the converted `.f3d` throws `AssetNotFoundException` with the generated path as its `key`, where it threw `StateError`. Catch `AssetNotFoundException` instead.
- <a id="flutter3d-package-flutter3d_core-formats-dart"></a>**`package:flutter3d_core/formats.dart`** (`dart fix`). Names re-exported from `package:flutter3d_core/formats.dart` are imported from `package:flutter3d_core/formats.dart`. `package:flutter3d/flutter3d.dart` re-exports the formats library through a `show` list now. The readers and writers a game calls are on it; the format internals (`F3dSection`, the KTX2 header fields, the material expression tree, the block encoders) are not. Import `package:flutter3d_core/formats.dart` for those; `dart fix` adds the import, and `migrate` the dependency on `flutter3d_core`.

### `flutter3d_addon_access`

- <a id="4b-addon_access-library"></a>**`package:flutter3d_addon_access/flutter3d_addon_access.dart`** (`migrate`). `package:flutter3d_addon_access/flutter3d_addon_access.dart` is `package:flutter3d_game_ui/access.dart`. `flutter3d_addon_access` is the `access.dart` library of `flutter3d_game_ui`, with the API it had; `package:flutter3d_game_ui/flutter3d_game_ui.dart` exports it beside its siblings.
- <a id="4b-addon_access-src"></a>**`package:flutter3d_addon_access/src/`** (`migrate`). `package:flutter3d_addon_access/src/` is `package:flutter3d_game_ui/src/access/`. What was under `flutter3d_addon_access`'s `lib/src/` is under `lib/src/access/` of `flutter3d_game_ui`. Importing it was never the public API; the main library above is.
