- <a id="core-format-exceptions"></a>**`readFmat`** (by hand). The readers in flutter3d_core throw their own leaf of `Flutter3dFormatException` instead of `dart:core`'s `FormatException`: `GltfFormatException` (glTF/GLB, `GlbContainer`, `decodeDataUri`), `FmatFormatException` (`readFmat`), `CubeLutFormatException` (`CubeLut.parse`), `MeshoptFormatException`, `FbxFormatException`, `MaterialBundleException` and `AnimationGraphFormatException` (`AnimationGraphJson.decode`). An `on FormatException` around one of them no longer catches: catch the leaf, or `Flutter3dFormatException` for all of them.
- <a id="4a-core-samplerOptionsFor"></a>**`samplerOptionsFor`** (nothing to do). Only a type its declaration names was renamed; that rename's own fix carries every use.

- <a id="4a-core-splatColour"></a>**`splatColour`** (`dart fix`). `splatColour` is `splatColor`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.

A package that compiles onto the engine's stages imports them from `package:flutter3d_cpu/builtin.dart`, which semver does not cover. The engine's `decodeOctahedral`, from `flutter3d_core`. The engine's `encodeOctahedral`, from `flutter3d_core`.
- <a id="4a-cpu-BoundTexture-sampler"></a>**`BoundTexture.sampler`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="cpu-CpuComputeShader"></a>**`CpuComputeShader`** (by hand). An `abstract base class` since 1.0: write `extends CpuComputeShader` where you wrote `implements CpuComputeShader`, mark the class `final`, `base` or `sealed`, and give a `const` constructor a `super()` it now has.
- <a id="f2-cpu-CpuDevice"></a>**`CpuDevice`** (by hand). What `CpuDevice` inherits from `GraphicsDevice` changed: the texture creators answer a non-null handle and throw `DeviceResourceException` or `UnsupportedCapability` where they answered null, `readPixels` is `readback`, and the deprecated `supportsX`, `maxAnisotropy` and `maxColorAttachments` are `features.has(...)` and `limits`. The entries for `GraphicsDevice` say each.
- <a id="4a-cpu-CpuDevice-createPipeline"></a>**`CpuDevice.createPipeline`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-cpu-CpuDevice-createTexture"></a>**`CpuDevice.createTexture`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="f2-cpu-CpuDevice-readPixels"></a>**`CpuDevice.readPixels`** (`dart fix`). `CpuDevice.readPixels` is `readback`.
- <a id="cpu-CpuEncoder"></a>**`CpuEncoder`** (by hand). A `CpuEncoder` is made by `CpuDevice.beginRenderPass`, which hands it the device's features, format support and timestamp clock. Code that constructed one directly opens the pass on a `CpuDevice` instead.
- <a id="cpu-CpuFragmentShader"></a>**`CpuFragmentShader`** (by hand). An `abstract base class` since 1.0: write `extends CpuFragmentShader` where you wrote `implements CpuFragmentShader`, mark the class `final`, `base` or `sealed`, and give a `const` constructor a `super()` it now has.
- <a id="cpu-CpuVertexShader"></a>**`CpuVertexShader`** (by hand). An `abstract base class` since 1.0: write `extends CpuVertexShader` where you wrote `implements CpuVertexShader`, mark the class `final`, `base` or `sealed`, and give a `const` constructor a `super()` it now has.
- <a id="cpu-CpuVertexShaderByIndex"></a>**`CpuVertexShaderByIndex`** (by hand). An `abstract base class` since 1.0: write `extends CpuVertexShaderByIndex` where you wrote `implements CpuVertexShaderByIndex`, mark the class `final`, `base` or `sealed`, and give a `const` constructor a `super()` it now has.
- <a id="4a-cpu-DepthOfFieldShader-circleOfConfusion"></a>**`DepthOfFieldShader.circleOfConfusion`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="cpu-UnlitShader"></a>**`UnlitShader`** (by hand). was the software rasteriser's own: one of the engine's shader stages in Dart, a helper they share, or the encoder. It is not exported since 1.0, so a change to the engine's shaders is not a major release. A stage of your own is written with the kit that stays — `CpuStage`, `CpuFragmentShader`, `CpuVertexShader`, `ShaderBindings`, `FragmentContext` — and a test that wanted an unlit stand-in uses `cpuUnlitStage` from `package:flutter3d_cpu/testing.dart`.
- <a id="cpu-VertexFetch"></a>**`VertexFetch`** (by hand). `VertexFetch` is closed: its two kinds are `LayoutFetch` and `PackedFetch`. Reading vertices through one is unchanged; a fetch of your own builds a `LayoutFetch` over its buffer layout instead.
- <a id="cpu-ensureCpuBackendRegistered"></a>**`ensureCpuBackendRegistered`** (by hand). There is no process-wide registry since 1.0: `registerCpuBackend(registry, materialCompiler: ...)` adds the software rasteriser to an engine's own `DeviceRegistry`, as its fallback. `flutter3d_app`'s `platformDevices()` does it for this platform.
- <a id="cpu-kMeshVaryings"></a>**`kMeshVaryings`** (nothing to do). Only the value of the constant moved; code that reads `kMeshVaryings` gets the new one. It is a version or a layout size the engine stamps and checks itself.

It was on pub.dev as `flutter3d_editor_mcp`.

What was under `flutter3d_editor_mcp`'s `lib/src/` is under `lib/src/editor/` of `flutter3d_mcp`. Importing it was never the public API; the main library above is.
- <a id="editor_mcp-EditorSession"></a>**`EditorSession`** (by hand). `save` returns a `Future<Answer>` now, because it writes the file asynchronously. `await` it where its answer was read.
- <a id="editor_mcp-EditorSession-root"></a>**`EditorSession`** (nothing to do). The constructor gained an optional `root:`. `save` and the capture tools refuse a path outside the level's project, and a save to the generated level under another spelling is refused like the plain one.

The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="game-DualAxisBinding"></a>**`DualAxisBinding`** (nothing to do). `ActionBinding` is new in 1.0, sealed with its five shapes from the start, and `DualAxisBinding` is one of them. No 0.8 code has a `switch` over `ActionBinding` to be missing a case.
- <a id="game-DualAxisComposite"></a>**`DualAxisComposite`** (nothing to do). `ActionBinding` is new in 1.0, sealed with its five shapes from the start, and `DualAxisComposite` is one of them. No 0.8 code has a `switch` over `ActionBinding` to be missing a case.
- <a id="game-FixtureAppearance"></a>**`FixtureAppearance`** (`migrate`). `FixtureAppearance` is a base mixin class: `implements FixtureAppearance` becomes `with FixtureAppearance`, on a `base` class.
- <a id="4a-game-GameConfig"></a>**`GameConfig`** (`dart fix`). `GameConfig` is `GameSettings`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.
- <a id="f4-game-GameConfig-value"></a>**`GameConfig`** (by hand). `GameSettings` (what `GameConfig` became) is a value: change it with `withVolume(AudioBus.music, v)`, `withValue(key, v)`, `withoutValue(key)` or `copyWith`, each of which returns a new one. A volume is `volumeOf(AudioBus.music)` where it was `volumeOf('music')`. A setting is a typed `SettingKey`: `settingOf('pad.look', fallback)` is `valueOf(GameSettingKeys.padLook)`, or `chosenValueOf(key) ?? fallback` for a default that is somebody else's (the system's reduce-motion); a game's own setting is `SettingKey<T>('mygame.name', fallback: ...)`. `bindings` is `actions?.buttons`: the controls are one `ActionMap`. `fromJson` takes `defaultActions:` and reads a file from before the envelope, moving its names to the namespaced ids.
- <a id="game-LightFixtureBuild"></a>**`LightFixtureBuild`** (nothing to do). Only the engine's material type in its signature changed: `Material` is `RenderMaterial` (`core-Material-RenderMaterial` renames it).
- <a id="b1-game-OpenKind"></a>**`OpenKind`** (`dart fix`). `OpenKind` is imported from `package:flutter3d_sim/flutter3d_sim.dart`. `OpenKind` is `flutter3d_sim`'s now, the same class the editor uses; `openRegistryFor` is unchanged.
- <a id="f4-game-PadAxisPair"></a>**`PadAxisPair`** (by hand). Bind each direction of the axis as a button with a magnitude: `InputSource.padHalfAxis('leftStickX', positive: false)` to the negative action and `positive: true` to the positive one, in the action map's buttons.
- <a id="game-PadInput"></a>**`PadInput`** (by hand). `PadInput` reads an `ActionMap`: pass `actions:` where `bindings:` was, and read `actions.buttons` where `bindings` was read. `addDefaultsTo` and `knowsPad` take the map (`addDefaultsTo(map)` returns it); `defaultBindings()` is `PadInput.addDefaultsTo(ActionMap(actions: ActionSet.common)).buttons`.
- <a id="f4-game-PadInput-pad"></a>**`PadInput`** (by hand). What the sticks do is the action map's (see `f4-game-PadRoutes`); `routes.lookRate` is `lookRate`. `slotButtons: PadInput.dpadSlots` is `PadInput.addSlotDefaultsTo(map)`, which binds the d-pad to `SlotActions`. `storeSettings` is gone: the settings panel writes the dead zone and the look speed as `GameSettingKeys`, which `applySettings` reads.
- <a id="game-PadPresses"></a>**`PadPresses`** (nothing to do). `offer` takes the `GameSettingsController` that `game-SettingsCubit` renames at the call site; nothing else changed.
- <a id="f4-game-PadRoutes"></a>**`PadRoutes`** (by hand). A pad's sticks are bindings in the action map. `PadRoutes.walking` is what `PadInput.addDefaultsTo(map)` binds (the left stick walks, the right one looks); `PadRoutes.driving(steerLeft:, steerRight:)` is `PadInput.addDrivingDefaultsTo(map, steerLeft:, steerRight:)`; `lookRate` is `PadInput.lookRate`. Pass no `routes:` to `PadInput`.
- <a id="game-PadStickTarget"></a>**`PadStickTarget`** (by hand). `PadStickTarget` is gone with `PadStickUse`: what a stick does is a `DualAxisBinding` on `InputSource.padStick('left')` or `'right'` in the action map, to `DualAxisAction.move`, `DualAxisAction.look` or a game's own `DualAxisAction`, which the game reads off the `InputState`.
- <a id="f4-game-PadStickUse"></a>**`PadStickUse`** (by hand). Bind the stick to an action instead: `DualAxisBinding(myAction, InputSource.padStick('left'))` in the action map, and read `input.dualAxis(myAction)` where the use's `route` was called. `PadStickUse.move` and `look` are `DualAxisAction.move` and `look`; `ignored` is no binding.
- <a id="game-Playing"></a>**`Playing`** (by hand). `Playing` is an object: make one with `Playing.ofPlatform()` where the game starts and read `touch`, `capturesPointer` and `dragLook` from it where the static getters were read. A test constructs `Playing(touch: …, capturesPointer: …)` instead of overriding the platform.
- <a id="4a-game-Playing-dragLook"></a>**`Playing.dragLook`** (`dart fix`). `Playing.dragLook` is `usesDragLook`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="game-Rebinding"></a>**`Rebinding`** (nothing to do). `bindings` went from required to optional, beside a new `actions` map and `onConflict`; `Rebinding(bindings: table)` means what it did. `Rebinding` is final, so nothing extends the constructor.
- <a id="game-RunSession"></a>**`RunSession`** (by hand). `save` returns whether it wrote the run. A call that ignored the result is unchanged; a subclass that overrides `save` declares it `bool save()` and returns `true` once the run is written.
- <a id="4a-game-RunSession-close"></a>**`RunSession.close`** (`dart fix`). `RunSession.close` is `disposeLevel`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-game-RunSession-open"></a>**`RunSession.open`** (`dart fix`). `RunSession.open` is `loadLevel`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="f1-timeline-RunTimeline-loop"></a>**`RunTimeline.RunTimeline`** (by hand). `RunTimeline(rewind:, input:, stepSim:, restore:)` is `RunTimeline(rewind:, loop:)`: the `EngineLoop` the run steps in, which the buffer is attached to (`rewind.attach(loop)`, in place of adding its recorder to `loop.recorders` and calling `keyframe` with the run's save). The run has to be a part of the loop's snapshots — a genre is, under its plugin id; a game's own run adds `loop.snapshots.add(SnapshotPart.of(id:, capture:, restore:))` — and its step a system of the loop's. `input` and `stepSeconds` are the loop's.
- <a id="f1-timeline-RunTimeline-input"></a>**`RunTimeline.input`** (nothing to do). Still readable: a getter for the loop's `input` where it was a field. Nothing outside the package implements `RunTimeline`.
- <a id="f1-timeline-RunTimeline-restore"></a>**`RunTimeline.restore`** (by hand). The timeline restores through the loop's snapshots (`loop.rewindTo(step, state:)`), so the run is a part of them — a genre's is, under its plugin id — rather than a function handed to the timeline.
- <a id="f1-timeline-RunTimeline-stepSeconds"></a>**`RunTimeline.stepSeconds`** (nothing to do). Still readable: a getter for the loop's `stepSeconds` where it was a field. Nothing outside the package implements `RunTimeline`.
- <a id="f1-timeline-RunTimeline-stepSim"></a>**`RunTimeline.stepSim`** (by hand). The timeline steps the loop it was given (`loop.runSteps`), so the run's step is a system of that loop, not a function handed to the timeline.
- <a id="f1-timeline-RunTimeline-tracks"></a>**`RunTimeline.tracks`** (by hand). `tracks(capture:, layout:)` is `tracks(layout:, part:)`: the loop's capture is read at each step, and `part:` — the run's part, a genre's plugin id — hands the layout that part's own data, which is what a layout written against the run's `save()` reads.
- <a id="game-SaveFile"></a>**`SaveFile`** (by hand). `read`, `readRecord`, `write`, `writeRecord` and `clear` are asynchronous, because `Storage` is: await them. A save kept before slots is the default slot's (`save.json`); `SaveSlots` holds more.
- <a id="game-ScrubMoved"></a>**`ScrubMoved`** (by hand). `ScrubAnswer` has a new case, `ScrubMoved`: a `switch` over a `ScrubAnswer` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="game-ScrubRefused"></a>**`ScrubRefused`** (by hand). `ScrubAnswer` has a new case, `ScrubRefused`: a `switch` over a `ScrubAnswer` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="f4-game-SettingsCubit-settings"></a>**`SettingsCubit`** (by hand). `GameSettingsController` takes `settings:` where it took `config:`, and the map the devices read as `actions:`. `settings` is the current value, replaced on every change; keep yours current in `apply`. `setVolume` takes an `AudioBus`, and `setSetting(name, value)` is `setValue(GameSettingKeys.<key>, value)`.
- <a id="game-SettingsCubit"></a>**`SettingsCubit`** (`dart fix`). `SettingsCubit` is `GameSettingsController`. `GameSettingsController` is the same state machine as a `ValueListenable` rather than a `Cubit`: read `.value` where `.state` was read, listen with a `ValueListenableBuilder` where a `BlocBuilder` was, and call `dispose()` where `close()` was. `rebind` takes any action and its `part` (`rebindAction` is gone), and `reset(defaults)` takes the action map where `resetControls` took a button table.
- <a id="game-SettingsFile"></a>**`SettingsFile`** (by hand). `read`, `write` and `clear` are asynchronous, because `Storage` is. Read the settings in `main()` before `runApp` (after `WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized()`) and hand the `GameConfig` to the screen, so the first key press uses them. `write` answers `Future<bool>`.
- <a id="game-SettingsOverlay"></a>**`SettingsOverlay`** (by hand). `SettingsOverlay` takes `settings`, `sections`, `opening` and `canOpen`. Pass `sections: SettingsSection.standard(buses: ..., defaultActions: ..., padConnected: ..., credits: ..., colors: ..., privacy: ...)` where `mixer`, `bindings`, `config`, `padConnected`, `defaultBindings`, `credits` and the rest were; the sections read and change the `GameSettingsController` themselves.
- <a id="game-SettingsOverlay-moved"></a>**`SettingsOverlay`** (`dart fix`). `SettingsOverlay` is imported from `package:flutter3d_game_ui/flutter3d_game_ui.dart`. The widgets left `flutter3d_game` for `flutter3d_game_ui`, which stands on it; `flutter3d_game` keeps the input, the run, the settings and the saves they show. `dart fix` adds the import, and `migrate` the dependency.
- <a id="game-SettingsPanel"></a>**`SettingsPanel`** (by hand). `SettingsPanel(settings: controller, sections: [...])` replaces the mixer, the config, the callbacks and the rest: the sections (`SettingsSection.standard(...)`, or `VolumesSection`, `ControlsSection`, `AccessibilitySection`, `MouseSection`, `GamepadSection` and their kin) read and change the `GameSettingsController`, and the panel closes through it.
- <a id="game-SettingsPanel-moved"></a>**`SettingsPanel`** (`dart fix`). `SettingsPanel` is imported from `package:flutter3d_game_ui/flutter3d_game_ui.dart`. The widgets left `flutter3d_game` for `flutter3d_game_ui`, which stands on it; `flutter3d_game` keeps the input, the run, the settings and the saves they show. `dart fix` adds the import, and `migrate` the dependency.
- <a id="game-SettingsState"></a>**`SettingsState`** (by hand). `waitingFor` is the action of any kind that is listening, an `InputAction<Object>?`; `waitingForAction` is gone into it. A check for a button compares with `is GameAction`.
- <a id="game-TapToRestart-moved"></a>**`TapToRestart`** (`dart fix`). `TapToRestart` is imported from `package:flutter3d_game_ui/flutter3d_game_ui.dart`. The widgets left `flutter3d_game` for `flutter3d_game_ui`, which stands on it; `flutter3d_game` keeps the input, the run, the settings and the saves they show. `dart fix` adds the import, and `migrate` the dependency.
- <a id="game-TapToRestart"></a>**`TapToRestart`** (nothing to do). `label` is nullable and defaults to the player's language; a label that was passed still shows, and one that was not reads "Tap to play again" in English.
- <a id="game-TimelineLevelSwapped"></a>**`TimelineLevelSwapped`** (by hand). `TimelineCommand` has a new case, `TimelineLevelSwapped`: a `switch` over a `TimelineCommand` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="game-TimelineReplayed"></a>**`TimelineReplayed`** (by hand). `TimelineCommand` has a new case, `TimelineReplayed`: a `switch` over a `TimelineCommand` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="game-TimelineReturned"></a>**`TimelineReturned`** (by hand). `TimelineCommand` has a new case, `TimelineReturned`: a `switch` over a `TimelineCommand` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="game-TimelineScrubbed"></a>**`TimelineScrubbed`** (by hand). `TimelineCommand` has a new case, `TimelineScrubbed`: a `switch` over a `TimelineCommand` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="game-TouchAction"></a>**`TouchAction`** (`dart fix`). `TouchAction` is imported from `package:flutter3d_game_ui/flutter3d_game_ui.dart`. The widgets left `flutter3d_game` for `flutter3d_game_ui`, which stands on it; `flutter3d_game` keeps the input, the run, the settings and the saves they show. `dart fix` adds the import, and `migrate` the dependency.
- <a id="game-TouchButton"></a>**`TouchButton`** (`dart fix`). `TouchButton` is imported from `package:flutter3d_game_ui/flutter3d_game_ui.dart`. The widgets left `flutter3d_game` for `flutter3d_game_ui`, which stands on it; `flutter3d_game` keeps the input, the run, the settings and the saves they show. `dart fix` adds the import, and `migrate` the dependency.
- <a id="game-TouchControls-moved"></a>**`TouchControls`** (`dart fix`). `TouchControls` is imported from `package:flutter3d_game_ui/flutter3d_game_ui.dart`. The widgets left `flutter3d_game` for `flutter3d_game_ui`, which stands on it; `flutter3d_game` keeps the input, the run, the settings and the saves they show. `dart fix` adds the import, and `migrate` the dependency.
- <a id="game-TouchControls"></a>**`TouchControls`** (nothing to do). `stickAction` is a new optional parameter, defaulting to walking as the stick always did. A widget is built, not implemented, so no caller has anything to do.
- <a id="game-TouchSlot"></a>**`TouchSlot`** (`dart fix`). `TouchSlot` is imported from `package:flutter3d_game_ui/flutter3d_game_ui.dart`. The widgets left `flutter3d_game` for `flutter3d_game_ui`, which stands on it; `flutter3d_game` keeps the input, the run, the settings and the saves they show. `dart fix` adds the import, and `migrate` the dependency.
- <a id="game-TouchSlots"></a>**`TouchSlots`** (`dart fix`). `TouchSlots` is imported from `package:flutter3d_game_ui/flutter3d_game_ui.dart`. The widgets left `flutter3d_game` for `flutter3d_game_ui`, which stands on it; `flutter3d_game` keeps the input, the run, the settings and the saves they show. `dart fix` adds the import, and `migrate` the dependency.
- <a id="game-TouchStick-moved"></a>**`TouchStick`** (`dart fix`). `TouchStick` is imported from `package:flutter3d_game_ui/flutter3d_game_ui.dart`. The widgets left `flutter3d_game` for `flutter3d_game_ui`, which stands on it; `flutter3d_game` keeps the input, the run, the settings and the saves they show. `dart fix` adds the import, and `migrate` the dependency.
- <a id="game-TouchStick"></a>**`TouchStick`** (nothing to do). `action` is a new optional parameter, defaulting to `DualAxisAction.move`, which writes exactly what the stick wrote. A widget is built, not implemented, so no caller has anything to do.
- <a id="game-TouchToggle"></a>**`TouchToggle`** (`dart fix`). `TouchToggle` is imported from `package:flutter3d_game_ui/flutter3d_game_ui.dart`. The widgets left `flutter3d_game` for `flutter3d_game_ui`, which stands on it; `flutter3d_game` keeps the input, the run, the settings and the saves they show. `dart fix` adds the import, and `migrate` the dependency.
- <a id="4a-game-applySavedVolumes"></a>**`applySavedVolumes`** (nothing to do). Only a type its declaration names was renamed; that rename's own fix carries every use.
- <a id="f1-timeline-bugReportTape"></a>**`bugReportTape`** (nothing to do). An attached buffer keeps the loop's captures; pass `part:` (the run's part, a genre's plugin id) so the report's start is the run's own snapshot, which a `.f3drun` holds. Without it the keyframe is handed over whole, which is right only for a buffer filled by hand.
- <a id="game-configureForTouch"></a>**`configureForTouch`** (`dart fix`). `configureForTouch` is `lockLandscapeForTouch`. Called from `main()` as before; it takes the game's `Playing` optionally and asks the platform when given none.
- <a id="game-creditGaps"></a>**`creditGaps`** (by hand). `creditGaps` checked this repository's demos against their asset folders and was not engine API; it lives in the repository's own test tooling now and no published package exports it. A test that used it copies it: it lists the asset folder with `dart:io` and compares the names with the credits' `file`s.
- <a id="game-ownedBindings"></a>**`ownedBindings`** (by hand). Read the map the devices share as `settings.actionsOr(defaults)` and hand that same map to `GameSettingsController(actions: ...)`, which saves a copy of it on every rebind; `SettingsFile(defaultActions: defaults)` reads a saved map against the game's own.
- <a id="game-package-flutter3d_game-testing-dart"></a>**`package:flutter3d_game/testing.dart`** (by hand). `creditGaps` checked this repository's demos against their asset folders and was not engine API. Copy it into the test that uses it: it lists the folder with `dart:io` and compares the names with the credits' `file`s.
- <a id="f1-timeline-registerTimelineExtensions"></a>**`registerTimelineExtensions`** (by hand). `capture:` is gone: `scrubTo` and `replayUnderNewCode` are registered always, through the timeline's loop. `tracks` is registered with `entityLayout:`, and reads the part `trackedPart:` names (a genre's plugin id) when the layout reads the run's own save.
- <a id="f3-game-registerTimelineExtensions-registration"></a>**`registerTimelineExtensions`** (nothing to do). It returns a `Registration`, which takes the timeline off the VM service, rather than `void`; a call that ignored the result compiles as before. A second timeline now registers beside the first instead of throwing.
- <a id="f1-timeline-replayDemo"></a>**`replayDemo`** (by hand). Replay through the loop: `replayDemoOnLoop(demo:, loop:, part:)`, where the loop steps the run (its genre installed, or the game's step as a system) and `part` is the run's part of its snapshots — the genre's plugin id (`PlatformerPlugin.id`), or the id of the `SnapshotPart` the game registered for its run. The file's start is restored as that part and each checkpoint digested from it, so recorded tapes check out as they did.
- <a id="f1-timeline-replayDemoOnLoop-part"></a>**`replayDemoOnLoop`** (by hand). `restore:` and `save:` are gone; pass `part:`, the id of the run's part of the loop's snapshots (a genre's plugin id, or the `SnapshotPart` the game registered for its run with its own `save` and `restore`). The loop is rewound to step nought with the file's start as that part.
- <a id="f1-timeline-rewindBufferFromDemo"></a>**`rewindBufferFromDemo`** (by hand). `rewindBufferFromDemo(demo:, stepsPerSecond:, input:, restore:, save:, stepSim:)` is `rewindBufferFromDemo(demo:, loop:, part:)`: the tape plays through the loop with the buffer attached, its start restored as the run's part. Steps per second are the loop's.
- <a id="game-settingsKeys"></a>**`settingsKeys`** (nothing to do). The settings argument is a `GameSettingsController`, which `game-SettingsCubit` renames at the call site; nothing else changed.

### `flutter3d_game_platformer`

- <a id="game_platformer-CrateKind"></a>**`CrateKind`** (by hand). The solver is typed as the `RigidDynamics` interface now, which the Dart `Dynamics` and the native solver both implement. Declare what you read it into as `RigidDynamics`; code that needs a member only the Dart solver has casts with `as Dynamics`.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-Crumbling-takeWeight"></a>**`Crumbling.takeWeight`** (`dart fix`). `Crumbling.takeWeight` is `bearWeight`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-FollowCamera-FollowCamera"></a>**`FollowCamera.FollowCamera`** (nothing to do). Only the type of the `tuning` default was renamed (`FollowTuning` is `FollowSettings`), and the parameters added beside it are optional; the rename's own fix carries every use of the type.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-FollowCamera-extraFov"></a>**`FollowCamera.extraFov`** (`dart fix`). `FollowCamera.extraFov` is `extraFovY`. Units in names: a field of view is `fovY`, the vertical angle, in radians (docs/CONTRACTS.md), and time in the simulation is a `double` of seconds. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-FollowCamera-follow"></a>**`FollowCamera.follow`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-FollowCamera-tuning"></a>**`FollowCamera.tuning`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-FollowTuning"></a>**`FollowTuning`** (`dart fix`). `FollowTuning` is `FollowSettings`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-FollowTuning-FollowTuning"></a>**`FollowTuning.FollowTuning`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-FollowTuning-recentre"></a>**`FollowTuning.recentre`** (`dart fix`). `FollowTuning.recentre` is `recenter`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-FollowTuning-recentreAbove"></a>**`FollowTuning.recentreAbove`** (`dart fix`). `FollowTuning.recentreAbove` is `recenterAbove`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="game_platformer-Gatherer"></a>**`Gatherer`** (`migrate`). `Gatherer` is a base mixin class: `implements Gatherer` becomes `with Gatherer`, on a `base` class.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-Hunter-hunting"></a>**`Hunter.hunting`** (`dart fix`). `Hunter.hunting` is `isHunting`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="game_platformer-KeyKind"></a>**`KeyKind`** (`dart fix`). `KeyKind` is `PlatformerKeyKind`. The shooter declares a `KeyKind` of its own, which spawns a different mechanism for the same `key` type, so each genre's is named for it.
- <a id="game_platformer-KeyTaker"></a>**`KeyTaker`** (`migrate`). `KeyTaker` is a base mixin class: `implements KeyTaker` becomes `with KeyTaker`, on a `base` class.
- <a id="game_platformer-Launchable"></a>**`Launchable`** (`migrate`). `Launchable` is a base mixin class: `implements Launchable` becomes `with Launchable`, on a `base` class.
- <a id="game_platformer-LivesStrip"></a>**`LivesStrip`** (nothing to do). Only the style's type was renamed; `game_platformer-ReadoutStyle` renames the `ReadoutStyle` a caller passes.
- <a id="game_platformer-PlatformerSimulation"></a>**`PlatformerSimulation`** (by hand). `dynamics` is a `RigidDynamics?` now, the interface the Dart and the native solver share. Declare what you read it into as `RigidDynamics?`, or cast with `as Dynamics?` for the Dart solver's own members.
- <a id="game_platformer-PlatformerSimulation-events"></a>**`PlatformerSimulation.events`** (by hand). The run's buffer is gone; it publishes onto the bus. With `PlatformerPlugin` installed nothing is to be done but subscribe. Before: `final events = sim.events.drain(); if (events.any((e) => e is RunnerDied)) camera.cut();` in the `publish` phase. After: `loop.onStepEnd((s) { if (s.events.any((e) => e is RunnerDied)) camera.cut(); })`, or `loop.events.onStep<RunnerDied>(...)`. Stepped by hand: `sim.publishTo(DirectBus()..onStep<GameEvent>('test', (d) => heard.add(d.event)))`.
- <a id="game_platformer-PurseReadout"></a>**`PurseReadout`** (nothing to do). Only the style's type was renamed; `game_platformer-ReadoutStyle` renames the `ReadoutStyle` a caller passes.
- <a id="game_platformer-ReadoutStyle"></a>**`ReadoutStyle`** (`dart fix`). `ReadoutStyle` is `PlatformerReadoutStyle`. The shooter and racing declare a `ReadoutStyle` each, with other fields, so each genre's is named for it.
- <a id="game_platformer-RunState"></a>**`RunState`** (by hand). `RunState` is a class with constants, so a state added in a minor release does not break an exhaustive `switch`. `RunState.running` and the rest, `values`, `name` and `outcome` read as before. A `switch` over a `RunState` needs a `default:` (or `_ =>`) case, `.index` is gone, and `RunState.values.byName(x)` is `RunState.byName(x)`, which answers null for a word it does not know.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-Runner-Runner"></a>**`Runner.Runner`** (nothing to do). Only the type of the `tuning` default was renamed (`RunnerTuning` is `RunnerSettings`), and the parameters added beside it are optional; the rename's own fix carries every use of the type.
- <a id="game_platformer-Runner-events"></a>**`Runner.events`** (by hand). `events` is an `EventRegistry?` the runner publishes its `RunnerEvent`s onto, set by `PlatformerSimulation.publishTo`. Before: `runner.events = buffer` and `buffer.drain()`. After: `runner.events = bus` (or, for a whole run, `sim.publishTo(bus)`), and a subscriber on the bus: `bus.onStep<Jumped>('game.jump', (d) => ...)`.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-Runner-tuning"></a>**`Runner.tuning`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-RunnerTuning"></a>**`RunnerTuning`** (`dart fix`). `RunnerTuning` is `RunnerSettings`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-Surfaces-Surfaces"></a>**`Surfaces.Surfaces`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-Surfaces-tuningFor"></a>**`Surfaces.tuningFor`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-SwimTuning"></a>**`SwimTuning`** (`dart fix`). `SwimTuning` is `SwimSettings`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-Water-Water"></a>**`Water.Water`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-game_platformer-Water-tuning"></a>**`Water.tuning`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="game_platformer-platformerRegistry"></a>**`platformerRegistry`** (nothing to do). `dynamics` takes a `RigidDynamics?`, which a `Dynamics` is: the parameter widened, so every call still fits.

### `flutter3d_game_racing`

- <a id="4a-game_racing-AiDriver-AiDriver"></a>**`AiDriver.AiDriver`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.

- <a id="4a-game_racing-ChaseCamera-ChaseCamera"></a>**`ChaseCamera.ChaseCamera`** (nothing to do). Only the type of the `tuning` default was renamed (`ChaseTuning` is `ChaseSettings`), and the parameters added beside it are optional; the rename's own fix carries every use of the type.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-ChaseCamera-fov"></a>**`ChaseCamera.fov`** (`dart fix`). `ChaseCamera.fov` is `fovY`. Units in names: a field of view is `fovY`, the vertical angle, in radians (docs/CONTRACTS.md), and time in the simulation is a `double` of seconds. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-ChaseCamera-tuning"></a>**`ChaseCamera.tuning`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-ChaseTuning"></a>**`ChaseTuning`** (`dart fix`). `ChaseTuning` is `ChaseSettings`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-ChaseTuning-ChaseTuning"></a>**`ChaseTuning.ChaseTuning`** (`dart fix`). Units in names: a field of view is `fovY`, the vertical angle, in radians (docs/CONTRACTS.md), and time in the simulation is a `double` of seconds. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-ChaseTuning-baseFov"></a>**`ChaseTuning.baseFov`** (`dart fix`). `ChaseTuning.baseFov` is `baseFovY`. Units in names: a field of view is `fovY`, the vertical angle, in radians (docs/CONTRACTS.md), and time in the simulation is a `double` of seconds. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-ChaseTuning-fovPerSpeed"></a>**`ChaseTuning.fovPerSpeed`** (`dart fix`). `ChaseTuning.fovPerSpeed` is `fovYPerSpeed`. Units in names: a field of view is `fovY`, the vertical angle, in radians (docs/CONTRACTS.md), and time in the simulation is a `double` of seconds. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="game_racing-GroundField"></a>**`GroundField`** (`migrate`). `GroundField` is a base mixin class: `implements GroundField` becomes `with GroundField`, on a `base` class.
- <a id="game_racing-LapReadout"></a>**`LapReadout`** (nothing to do). Only the style's type was renamed; `game_racing-ReadoutStyle` renames the `ReadoutStyle` a caller passes.
- <a id="game_racing-PositionReadout"></a>**`PositionReadout`** (nothing to do). Only the style's type was renamed; `game_racing-ReadoutStyle` renames the `ReadoutStyle` a caller passes.
- <a id="game_racing-RacePhase"></a>**`RacePhase`** (by hand). `RacePhase` is a class with constants, so the losing phase a race against the clock wants can arrive in a minor release without breaking an exhaustive `switch`. `RacePhase.countdown` and the rest, `values`, `name` and `outcome` read as before. A `switch` over a `RacePhase` needs a `default:` (or `_ =>`) case, `.index` is gone, and `RacePhase.values.byName(x)` is `RacePhase.byName(x)`, which answers null for a word it does not know.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-RacerProgress-finished"></a>**`RacerProgress.finished`** (`dart fix`). `RacerProgress.finished` is `isFinished`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="game_racing-RacingSimulation"></a>**`RacingSimulation`** (by hand). `dynamics` is a `RigidDynamics?` now, the interface the Dart and the native solver share. Declare what you read it into as `RigidDynamics?`, or cast with `as Dynamics?` for the Dart solver's own members.
- <a id="game_racing-RacingSimulation-events"></a>**`RacingSimulation.events`** (by hand). The race's buffer is gone; it publishes onto the bus. With `RacingPlugin` installed nothing is to be done but subscribe. Before: `for (final e in race.events.drain()) { if (e is LapCompleted && e.isPlayer) showLap(); }`. After: `loop.events.onStep<LapCompleted>('game.lap', (d) { if (d.event.isPlayer) showLap(); })`, or read `StepEventSummary.events` in `loop.onStepEnd`. Stepped by hand: `race.publishTo(DirectBus()..onStep<GameEvent>('test', (d) => heard.add(d.event)))`. `events.clear()` on a restart has nothing to clear any more.
- <a id="game_racing-ReadoutStyle"></a>**`ReadoutStyle`** (`dart fix`). `ReadoutStyle` is `RacingReadoutStyle`. The platformer and the shooter declare a `ReadoutStyle` each, with other fields, so each genre's is named for it.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-RoadMeshSettings-RoadMeshSettings"></a>**`RoadMeshSettings.RoadMeshSettings`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-RoadMeshSettings-metresPerTile"></a>**`RoadMeshSettings.metresPerTile`** (`dart fix`). `RoadMeshSettings.metresPerTile` is `metersPerTile`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-SkyPreset-angles"></a>**`SkyPreset`** (by hand). `sunElevationDeg` and `sunAzimuthDeg` are `sunElevation` and `sunAzimuth`, in radians, as every angle in the engine is (docs/CONTRACTS.md). Multiply a number in degrees by π/180 (`x * (math.pi / 180.0)`). A track file keeps its `sunElevationDeg` and `sunAzimuthDeg` keys in degrees; `SkyPreset.fromJson` converts.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-SkyPreset-colourAt"></a>**`SkyPreset.colourAt`** (`dart fix`). `SkyPreset.colourAt` is `colorAt`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_racing-SkyPreset-horizonFogColour"></a>**`SkyPreset.horizonFogColour`** (`dart fix`). `SkyPreset.horizonFogColour` is `horizonFogColor`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.

### `flutter3d_game_shooter`

- <a id="game_shooter-AmmoGift"></a>**`AmmoGift`** (by hand). `announce` returns a `GiftAnnouncement` (a message id with the gift, the amount and the detail) instead of an English sentence, so a game words it in its own language. `.english` is the sentence it used to return.
- <a id="game_shooter-AmmoReadout"></a>**`AmmoReadout`** (nothing to do). Only the style's type was renamed; `game_shooter-ReadoutStyle` renames the `ReadoutStyle` a caller passes.
- <a id="game_shooter-ArmourGift"></a>**`ArmourGift`** (by hand). `announce` returns a `GiftAnnouncement` instead of an English sentence; `.english` is the sentence it used to return.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-BlastResolver-resolve"></a>**`BlastResolver.resolve`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="game_shooter-Collector"></a>**`Collector`** (`migrate`). `Collector` is a base mixin class: `implements Collector` becomes `with Collector`, on a `base` class.
- <a id="game_shooter-GameSimulation"></a>**`GameSimulation`** (by hand). `dynamics` is a `RigidDynamics?` now, the interface the Dart and the native solver share. Declare what you read it into as `RigidDynamics?`, or cast with `as Dynamics?` for the Dart solver's own members.
- <a id="game_shooter-GameSimulation-events"></a>**`GameSimulation.events`** (by hand). The run's buffer is gone; it publishes onto the bus. With `ShooterPlugin` installed nothing is to be done but subscribe. Before: `final events = sim.events.drain(); if (events.any((e) => e is PlayerDied)) showDeath();` in the `publish` phase. After: `loop.events.onStep<PlayerDied>('game.died', (d) => showDeath())`, or read `StepEventSummary.events` in `loop.onStepEnd`. Stepped by hand: `sim.publishTo(DirectBus()..onStep<GameEvent>('test', (d) => heard.add(d.event)))`. Cutscene signals arrive on the same bus as `SequenceSignal`s, with `Cutscene.signals` gone: subscribe to `SequenceSignal` and switch on its `signal`.
- <a id="game_shooter-GameState"></a>**`GameState`** (by hand). `GameState` is a class with constants, so a state added in a minor release does not break an exhaustive `switch`. `GameState.playing` and the rest, `values`, `name` and `outcome` read as before. A `switch` over a `GameState` needs a `default:` (or `_ =>`) case, `.index` is gone, and `GameState.values.byName(x)` is `GameState.byName(x)`, which answers null for a word it does not know.
- <a id="game_shooter-Gift"></a>**`Gift`** (by hand). `announce` returns a `GiftAnnouncement?` instead of a `String?`. A gift of your own returns `GiftAnnouncement('<your id>', gift: name, amount: amount, detail: detail)` and words that id where it shows messages; a caller that showed the string shows `.english`, or words the id.
- <a id="game_shooter-HealthBar"></a>**`HealthBar`** (nothing to do). Only the style's type was renamed; `game_shooter-ReadoutStyle` renames the `ReadoutStyle` a caller passes.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-HealthBar-HealthBar"></a>**`HealthBar.HealthBar`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-HealthBar-armour"></a>**`HealthBar.armour`** (`dart fix`). `HealthBar.armour` is `armor`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-HealthBar-maxArmour"></a>**`HealthBar.maxArmour`** (`dart fix`). `HealthBar.maxArmour` is `maxArmor`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="game_shooter-HealthGift"></a>**`HealthGift`** (by hand). `announce` returns a `GiftAnnouncement` instead of an English sentence; `.english` is the sentence it used to return.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-HitscanBehaviour"></a>**`HitscanBehaviour`** (`dart fix`). `HitscanBehaviour` is `HitscanBehavior`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-Inventory-Inventory"></a>**`Inventory.Inventory`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-Inventory-addArmour"></a>**`Inventory.addArmour`** (`dart fix`). `Inventory.addArmour` is `addArmor`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-Inventory-maxArmour"></a>**`Inventory.maxArmour`** (`dart fix`). `Inventory.maxArmour` is `maxArmor`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="game_shooter-KeyGift"></a>**`KeyGift`** (by hand). `announce` returns a `GiftAnnouncement` instead of an English sentence; `.english` is the sentence it used to return.
- <a id="game_shooter-KeyKind"></a>**`KeyKind`** (`dart fix`). `KeyKind` is `ShooterKeyKind`. The platformer declares a `KeyKind` of its own, which spawns a different mechanism for the same `key` type, so each genre's is named for it.
- <a id="game_shooter-KeyPips"></a>**`KeyPips`** (nothing to do). Only the style's type was renamed; `game_shooter-ReadoutStyle` renames the `ReadoutStyle` a caller passes.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-KeyPips-KeyPips"></a>**`KeyPips.KeyPips`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-KeyPips-colours"></a>**`KeyPips.colours`** (`dart fix`). `KeyPips.colours` is `colors`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-MeleeBehaviour"></a>**`MeleeBehaviour`** (`dart fix`). `MeleeBehaviour` is `MeleeBehavior`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="game_shooter-PowerUpGift"></a>**`PowerUpGift`** (by hand). `announce` returns a `GiftAnnouncement` instead of an English sentence; `.english` is the sentence it used to return.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-ProjectileBehaviour"></a>**`ProjectileBehaviour`** (`dart fix`). `ProjectileBehaviour` is `ProjectileBehavior`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="game_shooter-ReadoutStyle"></a>**`ReadoutStyle`** (`dart fix`). `ReadoutStyle` is `ShooterReadoutStyle`. The platformer and racing declare a `ReadoutStyle` each, with other fields, so each genre's is named for it.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-ShotHit-struckSomething"></a>**`ShotHit.struckSomething`** (`dart fix`). `ShotHit.struckSomething` is `didStrikeSomething`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="game_shooter-Staged"></a>**`Staged`** (by hand). `Staged` takes the stage's light fixtures as a required `fixtures`. `stage` builds a `Staged` with them; code that constructs one by hand passes the list it built, or `const []` for a stage without any.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-WeaponBehaviour"></a>**`WeaponBehaviour`** (`dart fix`). `WeaponBehaviour` is `WeaponBehavior`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-WeaponDef-WeaponDef"></a>**`WeaponDef.WeaponDef`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-WeaponDef-behaviour"></a>**`WeaponDef.behaviour`** (`dart fix`). `WeaponDef.behaviour` is `behavior`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-WeaponDef-cooldown"></a>**`WeaponDef.cooldown`** (by hand). `cooldown` is a `double` of seconds now, the simulation's unit of time, and the `Duration` getter is gone; `cooldownSeconds` was the same number and is `cooldown` too. Where a `Duration` is needed at the Flutter boundary, build it there: `Duration(microseconds: (cooldown * 1e6).round())`.
- <a id="4a-game_shooter-WeaponDef-cooldownSeconds"></a>**`WeaponDef.cooldownSeconds`** (`dart fix`). `WeaponDef.cooldownSeconds` is `cooldown`. Units in names: a field of view is `fovY`, the vertical angle, in radians (docs/CONTRACTS.md), and time in the simulation is a `double` of seconds. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="game_shooter-package-flutter3d_game_shooter-sample-dart"></a>**`package:flutter3d_game_shooter/sample.dart`** (by hand). The shipped game's roster (`Monsters`, `Weapons`, `sampleGifts`, `sampleRegistry`, `sampleRules`, `sampleArsenal`) was one game's content and is no longer published: it lives in this repository's `flutter3d_demo_content`, which is not on pub.dev. Copy the definitions your game uses into your own code; every type they are made of (`MonsterDef`, `WeaponDef`, `MonsterKind`, `PickupKind`, `GiftRegistry`, `Arsenal`) is still the genre's.
- <a id="game_shooter-package-flutter3d_game_shooter-staging-dart"></a>**`package:flutter3d_game_shooter/staging.dart`** (by hand). `stage()`, `Staged`, `startingInventory` and `ShooterHeadlessGame` assembled this repository's shooter from its own roster and are no longer published: they live in `flutter3d_demo_content`, which is not on pub.dev. Copy `stage()` into your game and hand it your registry and inventory; `ShooterPlugin(headless: ...)` takes your own `HeadlessGame`.

- <a id="4a-game_strategy-FogOfWar-centreOf"></a>**`FogOfWar.centreOf`** (`dart fix`). `FogOfWar.centreOf` is `centerOf`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_strategy-FogOfWar-centreX"></a>**`FogOfWar.centreX`** (`dart fix`). `FogOfWar.centreX` is `centerX`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_strategy-FogOfWar-centreZ"></a>**`FogOfWar.centreZ`** (`dart fix`). `FogOfWar.centreZ` is `centerZ`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-game_strategy-MapCamera-MapCamera"></a>**`MapCamera.MapCamera`** (nothing to do). Only the type of the `tuning` default was renamed (`MapCameraTuning` is `MapCameraSettings`), and the parameters added beside it are optional; the rename's own fix carries every use of the type.
- <a id="4a-game_strategy-MapCamera-tuning"></a>**`MapCamera.tuning`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-game_strategy-MapCameraTuning"></a>**`MapCameraTuning`** (`dart fix`). `MapCameraTuning` is `MapCameraSettings`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.
- <a id="game_strategy-MatchDemo"></a>**`MatchDemo`** (nothing to do). Only the value of the constant moved; code that reads `MatchDemo` gets the new one. It is a version or a layout size the engine stamps and checks itself.
- <a id="game_strategy-MoveOrder-obey-StrategyUnit"></a>**`MoveOrder.obey`** (nothing to do). The signature names `StrategyUnit`, the new name of `Unit` (`game_strategy-Unit`); `dart fix` renames the type where the caller spells it, and a call is unchanged.
- <a id="game_strategy-OrderQueue-assign-StrategyUnit"></a>**`OrderQueue.assign`** (nothing to do). The signature names `StrategyUnit`, the new name of `Unit` (`game_strategy-Unit`); `dart fix` renames the type where the caller spells it, and a call is unchanged.
- <a id="game_strategy-OrderQueue-attackWith-StrategyUnit"></a>**`OrderQueue.attackWith`** (nothing to do). The signature names `StrategyUnit`, the new name of `Unit` (`game_strategy-Unit`); `dart fix` renames the type where the caller spells it, and a call is unchanged.
- <a id="game_strategy-OrderQueue-moveTo-StrategyUnit"></a>**`OrderQueue.moveTo`** (nothing to do). The signature names `StrategyUnit`, the new name of `Unit` (`game_strategy-Unit`); `dart fix` renames the type where the caller spells it, and a call is unchanged.
- <a id="4a-game_strategy-ResourceNode-take"></a>**`ResourceNode.take`** (`dart fix`). `ResourceNode.take` is `harvest`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="f1-rename-strategy-Selection"></a>**`Selection`** (`dart fix`). `Selection` is `UnitSelection`. The strategy's ray-and-rectangle picking is `UnitSelection`, so it no longer shares a name with the modeller's element `Selection` in `flutter3d_mesh`.
- <a id="game_strategy-Squad-units-StrategyUnit"></a>**`Squad.units`** (nothing to do). The signature names `StrategyUnit`, the new name of `Unit` (`game_strategy-Unit`); `dart fix` renames the type where the caller spells it, and a call is unchanged.
- <a id="game_strategy-StrategyOrder-obey-StrategyUnit"></a>**`StrategyOrder.obey`** (nothing to do). The signature names `StrategyUnit`, the new name of `Unit` (`game_strategy-Unit`); `dart fix` renames the type where the caller spells it, and a call is unchanged.
- <a id="game_strategy-StrategySimulation-add-StrategyUnit"></a>**`StrategySimulation.add`** (nothing to do). The signature names `StrategyUnit`, the new name of `Unit` (`game_strategy-Unit`); `dart fix` renames the type where the caller spells it, and a call is unchanged.
- <a id="game_strategy-StrategySimulation-units-StrategyUnit"></a>**`StrategySimulation.units`** (nothing to do). The signature names `StrategyUnit`, the new name of `Unit` (`game_strategy-Unit`); `dart fix` renames the type where the caller spells it, and a call is unchanged.
- <a id="game_strategy-TrainOrder-obey-StrategyUnit"></a>**`TrainOrder.obey`** (nothing to do). The signature names `StrategyUnit`, the new name of `Unit` (`game_strategy-Unit`); `dart fix` renames the type where the caller spells it, and a call is unchanged.
- <a id="game_strategy-Unit"></a>**`Unit`** (`dart fix`). `Unit` is `StrategyUnit`. `Unit` is the foundation's unit of measurement since 1.0, and a public name has one home, so a unit on the map is a `StrategyUnit`. Nothing else about it changed.
- <a id="game_strategy-UnitOrder-UnitOrder-attack-StrategyUnit"></a>**`UnitOrder.UnitOrder.attack`** (nothing to do). The signature names `StrategyUnit`, the new name of `Unit` (`game_strategy-Unit`); `dart fix` renames the type where the caller spells it, and a call is unchanged.
- <a id="game_strategy-UnitOrder-target-StrategyUnit"></a>**`UnitOrder.target`** (nothing to do). The signature names `StrategyUnit`, the new name of `Unit` (`game_strategy-Unit`); `dart fix` renames the type where the caller spells it, and a call is unchanged.

### `flutter3d_hardware`

- <a id="hardware-BackendRegistration"></a>**`BackendRegistration`** (by hand). `DeviceRegistry.addBackend` hands back a `Registration`; `undo()` is `cancel()`.
- <a id="hardware-BindUniformBlockObject"></a>**`BindUniformBlockObject`** (`dart fix`). `BindUniformBlockObject` is imported from `package:flutter3d_hardware/flutter3d_hardware.dart`.
- <a id="hardware-BlendFactor"></a>**`BlendFactor`** (by hand). `BlendFactor` has new values (`source1Color`, `oneMinusSource1Color`, `source1Alpha`, `oneMinusSource1Alpha`): a `switch` over a `BlendFactor` that names every value needs a case for each, or a wildcard.
- <a id="hardware-BlendOperation"></a>**`BlendOperation`** (by hand). `BlendOperation` has new values (`min`, `max`): a `switch` over a `BlendOperation` that names every value needs a case for each, or a wildcard.
- <a id="hardware-CommandEncoder"></a>**`CommandEncoder`** (by hand). A `base mixin` on `PassEncoder` since 1.0: a backend writes `extends PassEncoder with CommandEncoder` where it wrote `implements CommandEncoder`. New members of `PassEncoder` (debug groups) have bodies.
- <a id="hardware-ComputeEncoder"></a>**`ComputeEncoder`** (by hand). Code that dispatches compute is unchanged: the new members and the optional `offsetInBytes`/`sizeInBytes` of `bindStorageBuffer` are additions to call. Only a backend of your own that implements `ComputeEncoder` has work: it adds the new members, and for a capability it lacks throws `UnsupportedCapability` and leaves the feature out of `features`, as the hardware interface section of CONTRIBUTING.md describes.
- <a id="hardware-ComputePipelineHandle"></a>**`ComputePipelineHandle`** (by hand). A handle has no public constructor since 1.0: a device hands one out. A backend wraps its own object with `wrapComputePipeline` from `package:flutter3d_hardware/backend.dart`, naming itself as `owner` so the handle's `dispose` gives it back; a test's fake does the same.
- <a id="f2-hardware-DeviceCapabilityForwarders"></a>**`DeviceCapabilityForwarders`** (by hand). The deprecated capability getters are gone from `GraphicsDevice`, and so is the mixin that answered them. Drop `with DeviceCapabilityForwarders` and ask `features`, `limits` and `textureFormatSupport` directly (the getters' own migrations name the rewrite for each).
- <a id="f2-hardware-DeviceRegistry-open"></a>**`DeviceRegistry.open`** (nothing to do). The signature is the same; what it throws when no backend starts is a `DeviceUnavailableException` (a `ResourceException`) rather than a `StateError`, with each backend's refusal in `refusals`. Code that caught `StateError` there catches `DeviceUnavailableException`.
- <a id="f2-hardware-DeviceRegistry-presenterFor"></a>**`DeviceRegistry.presenterFor`** (nothing to do). Matched with `device is T` rather than by exact runtime type, so a subclass of a registered device finds its presenter; nothing a caller writes changes.
- <a id="f2-hardware-FakeBackend"></a>**`FakeBackend`** (by hand). What `FakeBackend` inherits from `GraphicsDevice` changed: the texture creators answer a non-null handle and throw where they answered null, `readPixels` is `readback`, and the deprecated `supportsX`, `maxAnisotropy` and `maxColorAttachments` are `features.has(...)` and `limits`. The entries for `GraphicsDevice` say each.
- <a id="4a-hardware-FakeBackend-createPipeline"></a>**`FakeBackend.createPipeline`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-hardware-FakeBackend-createTexture"></a>**`FakeBackend.createTexture`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-hardware-FakeBackend-createdTextures"></a>**`FakeBackend.createdTextures`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-hardware-FakeBackend-disposed"></a>**`FakeBackend.disposed`** (`dart fix`). `FakeBackend.disposed` is `isDisposed`. `disposed` was a public field anybody could set; it is the read-only getter `isDisposed`, and only `dispose()` sets it.
- <a id="4a-hardware-FakeBackend-uploadedTextures"></a>**`FakeBackend.uploadedTextures`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-hardware-FakePass-flags"></a>**`FakePass`** (by hand). No positional `bool` in 1.0: `setDepthWrite(false)` is `setDepthWrite(enabled: false)`, and the same for `setDepthClamp` and `setAlphaToCoverage`, on every pass encoder. A backend that overrides them takes `{required bool enabled}`.
- <a id="hardware-GeometryBuffer"></a>**`GeometryBuffer`** (by hand). A handle has no public constructor since 1.0: a device hands one out. A backend wraps its own object with `wrapGeometry` from `package:flutter3d_hardware/backend.dart`, naming itself as `owner` so the handle's `dispose` gives it back; a test's fake does the same.
- <a id="hardware-GraphicsDevice"></a>**`GraphicsDevice`** (by hand). Code that draws through a device is unchanged; its capability questions moved to `features`, `limits` and `textureFormatSupport` (the `supportsX` getters are deprecated, and `dart run flutter3d_build:migrate` rewrites them). A backend of your own that implements `GraphicsDevice` mixes in `DeviceCapabilityForwarders` for the deprecated getters, adds the new members, and throws `UnsupportedCapability` for what it cannot do yet.
- <a id="hardware-GraphicsDevice-base"></a>**`GraphicsDevice`** (by hand). An `abstract base class` since 1.0, so a member added in a minor breaks no backend: write `extends GraphicsDevice` where you wrote `implements GraphicsDevice`, mark the class `final`, `base` or `sealed`, and drop `DeviceCapabilityForwarders` (the base class answers the deprecated getters). Feature-gated members have bodies that throw `UnsupportedCapability`; override what your backend has. New: `createPipelineAsync`, `lost`, `setLabel`, `releasePipeline`. A capability mixin such as `EncodedImageUpload` is `with`, not `implements`.
- <a id="f2-hardware-GraphicsDevice-createCubeRenderTarget"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.createCubeRenderTarget`** (by hand). Returns a `TextureHandle`, not a nullable one: a device without cubes throws `UnsupportedCapability` — ask `features.has(DeviceFeature.cubeTextures)` first.
- <a id="f2-hardware-GraphicsDevice-createCubeTextureFromPixels"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.createCubeTextureFromPixels`** (by hand). Returns a `TextureHandle`, not a nullable one: a device without cubes throws `UnsupportedCapability` (ask `features.has(DeviceFeature.cubeTextures)` first), and faces of the wrong count or size a `DeviceResourceException`.
- <a id="f2-hardware-GraphicsDevice-createTextureFromPixels"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.createTextureFromPixels`** (by hand). Returns a `TextureHandle`, not a nullable one: pixels that are not the size the description needs throw a `DeviceResourceException`. Drop the `!`; where the null was a "texture left out", catch `DeviceResourceException` there.
- <a id="hardware-maxAnisotropy"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.maxAnisotropy`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.maxAnisotropy` becomes `x.limits.maxSamplerAnisotropy`.
- <a id="hardware-maxColorAttachments"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.maxColorAttachments`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.maxColorAttachments` becomes `x.limits.maxColorAttachments`.
- <a id="f2-hardware-GraphicsDevice-readBufferSync"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.readBufferSync`** (by hand). A synchronous buffer read is a capability a device opts into, the `SynchronousBufferReadback` mixin, since WebGPU has no such call. Ask `device is SynchronousBufferReadback` (or for `DeviceFeature.synchronousReadback`) and call `readBufferSync` on the device as that type; a backend that implements it mixes the mixin in.
- <a id="f2-hardware-GraphicsDevice-readPixels"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.readPixels`** (`dart fix`). `GraphicsDevice.readPixels` is `readback`. `readPixels` and `readback` were two calls for the same bytes. `readback` is the one left: a whole texture in a float format is converted as `readPixels` converted it, and a texture that cannot be read throws (`ArgumentError`, or `DeviceResourceException`) where `readPixels` answered null, so a `!` after the call or a null check on its result can go.
- <a id="hardware-supportsAlphaToCoverage"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsAlphaToCoverage`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsAlphaToCoverage` becomes `x.features.has(DeviceFeature.alphaToCoverage)`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsBlendColor"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsBlendColor`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsBlendColor` becomes `x.features.has(DeviceFeature.blendConstant)`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsCompute"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsCompute`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsCompute` becomes `x.features.has(DeviceFeature.compute)`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsCubeTextures"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsCubeTextures`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsCubeTextures` becomes `x.features.has(DeviceFeature.cubeTextures)`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsFloat32Filtering"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsFloat32Filtering`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsFloat32Filtering` becomes `(x.features.has(DeviceFeature.float32Filterable) && x.features.has(DeviceFeature.float32Renderable))`. The old getter answered two questions at once; the rewrite asks both. Keep only the one the code needs: sampling with a linear filter is `float32Filterable`, drawing into the texture `float32Renderable`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsGpuTimestamps"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsGpuTimestamps`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsGpuTimestamps` becomes `x.features.has(DeviceFeature.gpuTimestamps)`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsIndependentBlend"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsIndependentBlend`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsIndependentBlend` becomes `x.features.has(DeviceFeature.independentBlend)`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsMipmaps"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsMipmaps`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsMipmaps` becomes `x.features.has(DeviceFeature.manualMipmaps)`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsOffscreenMsaa"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsOffscreenMsaa`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsOffscreenMsaa` becomes `x.features.has(DeviceFeature.offscreenMultisample)`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsRenderToMip"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsRenderToMip`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsRenderToMip` becomes `x.features.has(DeviceFeature.renderToMipLevel)`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsStencil"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsStencil`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsStencil` becomes `x.features.has(DeviceFeature.stencil)`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsTextureFormat"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsTextureFormat`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsTextureFormat` becomes `x.textureFormatSupport({0}).sampled`.
- <a id="hardware-supportsWireframe"></a>**`GraphicsDevice.supportsWireframe`** (`migrate`). `GraphicsDevice.supportsWireframe` becomes `x.features.has(DeviceFeature.wireframe)`.
- <a id="hardware-LayeredShaderLibrary"></a>**`LayeredShaderLibrary`** (`dart fix`). `LayeredShaderLibrary` is imported from `package:flutter3d_hardware/flutter3d_hardware.dart`.
- <a id="hardware-LoadedShaderLibrary"></a>**`LoadedShaderLibrary`** (by hand). A `base mixin` on `ShaderLibrary` since 1.0: write `with ShaderLibrary, LoadedShaderLibrary` where you wrote `implements LoadedShaderLibrary`.
- <a id="hardware-PassEncoder-base"></a>**`PassEncoder`** (by hand). An `abstract base mixin class` since 1.0: an encoder writes `extends PassEncoder with CommandEncoder` (or `with RenderBundleEncoder`) where it wrote `implements CommandEncoder`. The debug groups (`pushDebugGroup`, `popDebugGroup`, `insertDebugMarker`) have bodies that do nothing.
- <a id="hardware-PassEncoder"></a>**`PassEncoder`** (by hand). Code that records a pass is unchanged: `draw` gained the optional `firstIndex` and `indexCount`, and the new members are additions to call. Only an encoder of your own that implements `PassEncoder` has work: it adds the new members, throwing `UnsupportedCapability` for what its backend lacks.
- <a id="hardware-PassStateApply"></a>**`PassStateApply`** (`dart fix`). `PassStateApply` is imported from `package:flutter3d_hardware/flutter3d_hardware.dart`.
- <a id="hardware-PipelineHandle"></a>**`PipelineHandle`** (by hand). A handle has no public constructor since 1.0: a device hands one out. A backend wraps its own object with `wrapPipeline` from `package:flutter3d_hardware/backend.dart`, naming itself as `owner` so the handle's `dispose` gives it back; a test's fake does the same.
- <a id="hardware-PresenterRegistration"></a>**`PresenterRegistration`** (by hand). `DeviceRegistry.addPresenter` hands back a `Registration`; `undo()` is `cancel()`.
- <a id="hardware-RecordedAlphaToCoverage"></a>**`RecordedAlphaToCoverage`** (by hand). `Recorded` has a new case, `RecordedAlphaToCoverage`: a `switch` over a `Recorded` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="hardware-RecordedCall"></a>**`RecordedCall`** (by hand). `Recorded` has a new case, `RecordedCall`: a `switch` over a `Recorded` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="4a-hardware-RecordedTexture-sampler"></a>**`RecordedTexture.sampler`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="f2-hardware-RecordingDevice"></a>**`RecordingDevice`** (by hand). What `RecordingDevice` inherits from `GraphicsDevice` changed: the texture creators answer a non-null handle and throw where they answered null, `readPixels` is `readback`, and the deprecated `supportsX`, `maxAnisotropy` and `maxColorAttachments` are `features.has(...)` and `limits`. The entries for `GraphicsDevice` say each.
- <a id="4a-hardware-RecordingDevice-createPipeline"></a>**`RecordingDevice.createPipeline`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-hardware-RecordingDevice-createTexture"></a>**`RecordingDevice.createTexture`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="f2-hardware-RenderTargetDescriptor"></a>**`RenderTargetDescriptor`** (nothing to do). A `TextureDescriptor` now (it extends it), with the same constructor, fields and equality, so `createTexture` takes either.
- <a id="hardware-RenderTargetPool"></a>**`RenderTargetPool`** (nothing to do). `trim` returns the bytes it handed back instead of nothing. A call that ignores the result is unchanged, and `RenderTargetPool` is final, so nothing overrides it.
- <a id="4a-hardware-RenderTargetSpec"></a>**`RenderTargetSpec`** (`dart fix`). `RenderTargetSpec` is `RenderTargetDescriptor`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-hardware-SamplerOptions"></a>**`SamplerOptions`** (`dart fix`). `SamplerOptions` is `SamplerDescriptor`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.
- <a id="hardware-ShaderBundleRefused"></a>**`ShaderBundleRefused`** (`dart fix`). `ShaderBundleRefused` is `ShaderBundleException`. `ShaderBundleRefused` is `ShaderBundleException` (decision H: a type that is thrown is named `*Exception`), with the same members. It now extends `ResourceException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly, so `on Exception` still catches it, and it carries what `StaleShaderBundle` (new since 0.8.5, gone in 1.0) carried: `asset`, `refreshing` and `advice` for a stale bundle. `dart fix` renames every use.
- <a id="hardware-ShaderHandle"></a>**`ShaderHandle`** (by hand). A handle has no public constructor since 1.0: a device hands one out. A backend wraps its own object with `wrapShader` from `package:flutter3d_hardware/backend.dart`, naming itself as `owner` so the handle's `dispose` gives it back; a test's fake does the same.
- <a id="hardware-ShaderLibrary"></a>**`ShaderLibrary`** (by hand). An `abstract base mixin class` since 1.0: write `with ShaderLibrary` (or `extends ShaderLibrary`) where you wrote `implements ShaderLibrary`.
- <a id="hardware-StencilFace"></a>**`StencilFace`** (`dart fix`). `StencilFace` is imported from `package:flutter3d_hardware/flutter3d_hardware.dart`.
- <a id="hardware-StorageBuffer"></a>**`StorageBuffer`** (by hand). A handle has no public constructor since 1.0: a device hands one out. A backend wraps its own object with `wrapStorageBuffer` from `package:flutter3d_hardware/backend.dart`, naming itself as `owner` so the handle's `dispose` gives it back; a test's fake does the same.
- <a id="hardware-TextureAllocator"></a>**`TextureAllocator`** (by hand). An `abstract base mixin class` since 1.0: write `with TextureAllocator` where you wrote `implements TextureAllocator`. A `GraphicsDevice` already is one.
- <a id="f2-hardware-TextureAllocator-createTexture"></a>**`TextureAllocator.createTexture`** (by hand). An allocator (a backend, a test's fake) overrides `createTexture(TextureDescriptor descriptor)` now. Make the 2D target it always made when `descriptor is RenderTargetDescriptor`, and the general form (what `createTextureWithDescriptor` made) otherwise, or refuse it with `UnsupportedCapability(DeviceFeature.textureWrites, …)`.
- <a id="hardware-TextureBlockLayout"></a>**`TextureBlockLayout`** (`dart fix`). `TextureBlockLayout` is imported from `package:flutter3d_hardware/flutter3d_hardware.dart`.
- <a id="hardware-TextureFormat-open"></a>**`TextureFormat`** (by hand). An open set since 1.0, so a format a later minor adds breaks no `switch`: one over a `TextureFormat` needs a `_ =>` case. `values`, `name` (the wire name a file stores) and `index` are where they were; `TextureFormat.byName` replaces `values.byName` and `asNameMap`.
- <a id="hardware-TextureFormat"></a>**`TextureFormat`** (by hand). `TextureFormat` has new values (`r8g8b8a8SNormInt`, `r8g8b8a8UInt`, `r8g8b8a8SInt`, `r16Float`, `r16g16Float`, `r16g16b16a16UInt`, `r16g16b16a16SInt`, `r32UInt`, `r32SInt`, `r32g32Float`, `r32g32UInt`, `r32g32SInt`, `r32g32b32a32UInt`, `r32g32b32a32SInt`, `r10g10b10a2UNormInt`, `r11g11b10UFloat`, `r9g9b9e5UFloat`, `d16UNormInt`, `d32Float`): a `switch` over a `TextureFormat` that names every value needs a case for each, or a wildcard.
- <a id="hardware-TextureFormatCompression"></a>**`TextureFormatCompression`** (`dart fix`). `TextureFormatCompression` is imported from `package:flutter3d_hardware/flutter3d_hardware.dart`.
- <a id="hardware-TextureHandle"></a>**`TextureHandle`** (by hand). A handle has no public constructor since 1.0: a device hands one out. A backend wraps its own object with `wrapTexture` from `package:flutter3d_hardware/backend.dart`, naming itself as `owner` so the handle's `dispose` gives it back; a test's fake does the same.
- <a id="4a-hardware-TraceBindTexture-sampler"></a>**`TraceBindTexture.sampler`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-hardware-TraceBlobWriter-take"></a>**`TraceBlobWriter.take`** (`dart fix`). `TraceBlobWriter.take` is `drain`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-hardware-TraceCreatePipeline-layout"></a>**`TraceCreatePipeline.layout`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-hardware-TraceCreateTexture-spec"></a>**`TraceCreateTexture.spec`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="f2-hardware-TraceEvent"></a>**`TraceEvent`** (nothing to do). `TraceEvent` and `TracePassEvent` are `abstract base` rather than `sealed`, so a HAL call added in a 1.x minor adds an event without a break. A `switch` over them needs a `default` branch now (the analyzer says where); the replay's default throws naming the event's kind.
- <a id="hardware-TraceSetAlphaToCoverage"></a>**`TraceSetAlphaToCoverage`** (by hand). `TracePassEvent` has a new case, `TraceSetAlphaToCoverage`: a `switch` over a `TracePassEvent` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="hardware-VertexFormat"></a>**`VertexFormat`** (by hand). An open set since 1.0: a `switch` over it needs a `_ =>` case. `values`, `name`, `index`, `bytesPerElement`, `componentCount` and `byName` are there.
- <a id="4a-hardware-VertexLayoutSpec"></a>**`VertexLayoutSpec`** (`dart fix`). `VertexLayoutSpec` is `VertexLayoutDescriptor`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.
- <a id="hardware-lookUpDevicePresenter"></a>**`lookUpDevicePresenter`** (by hand). `registry.presenterFor(device)` on the `DeviceRegistry` the device came from.
- <a id="hardware-openRegisteredDevice"></a>**`openRegisteredDevice`** (by hand). `registry.open(width:, height:)` on the engine's `DeviceRegistry`, or `openDevice(...)` from `flutter3d_app`.
- <a id="hardware-registerBackendOpener"></a>**`registerBackendOpener`** (by hand). Backends are added to an engine's own `DeviceRegistry` since 1.0: `registry.addBackend(name, open, asFallback: ...)`, opened with `registry.open(width:, height:)`. A backend package's `register*Backend(registry)` does it for you; `flutter3d_app`'s `platformDevices()` makes one with this platform's.
- <a id="hardware-registerDevicePresenter"></a>**`registerDevicePresenter`** (by hand). `registry.addPresenter<T>(presenter)` on the engine's `DeviceRegistry`.
