id="impeller-forgetDefaultColorFormat"></a>`forgetDefaultColorFormat`, <a id="impeller-impellerSectionOf"></a>`impellerSectionOf`, <a id="impeller-runningSdk"></a>`runningSdk`. An application opens the backend with `GpuRenderBackend.create` or adds it to a `DeviceRegistry` with `registerGpuBackend`; `GpuRenderBackend.frameImage` gives a frame as a `dart:ui` image.
- <a id="f2-impeller-GpuRenderBackend"></a>**`GpuRenderBackend`** (by hand). What `GpuRenderBackend` inherits from `GraphicsDevice` changed: the texture creators answer a non-null handle and throw where they answered null, `readPixels` is `readback`, and the deprecated `supportsX`, `maxAnisotropy` and `maxColorAttachments` are `features.has(...)` and `limits`. The entries for `GraphicsDevice` say each.
- <a id="4a-impeller-GpuRenderBackend-create"></a>**`GpuRenderBackend.create`** (`dart fix`). `GpuRenderBackend.create` is `open`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-impeller-GpuRenderBackend-createPipeline"></a>**`GpuRenderBackend.createPipeline`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-impeller-GpuRenderBackend-createTexture"></a>**`GpuRenderBackend.createTexture`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="f2-impeller-GpuRenderBackend-debugReadbackStagingCount"></a>**`GpuRenderBackend.debugReadbackStagingCount`** (nothing to do). See `GpuRenderBackend.emplace`.
- <a id="f2-impeller-GpuRenderBackend-debugTransientRecreations"></a>**`GpuRenderBackend.debugTransientRecreations`** (nothing to do). See `GpuRenderBackend.emplace`.
- <a id="f2-impeller-GpuRenderBackend-internals"></a>**`GpuRenderBackend.emplace`** (nothing to do). `emplace`, `granule`, `noteRejectedSubmission`, `debugReadbackStagingCount` and `debugTransientRecreations` were the backend's own plumbing between its device and its encoder, with flutter_gpu's `BufferView` in a public signature; they are not exported since 1.0, and nothing outside the backend has a use for them.
- <a id="f2-impeller-GpuRenderBackend-granule"></a>**`GpuRenderBackend.granule`** (nothing to do). See `GpuRenderBackend.emplace`.
- <a id="f2-impeller-GpuRenderBackend-noteRejectedSubmission"></a>**`GpuRenderBackend.noteRejectedSubmission`** (nothing to do). See `GpuRenderBackend.emplace`.
- <a id="f2-impeller-GpuRenderBackend-readBufferSync"></a>**`GpuRenderBackend.readBufferSync`** (nothing to do). It only ever refused: a flutter_gpu `DeviceBuffer` has no read path, and the device is not a `SynchronousBufferReadback`.
- <a id="f2-impeller-GpuRenderBackend-readPixels"></a>**`GpuRenderBackend.readPixels`** (`dart fix`). `GpuRenderBackend.readPixels` is `readback`.
- <a id="impeller-ensureGpuBackendRegistered"></a>**`ensureGpuBackendRegistered`** (by hand). There is no process-wide registry since 1.0: `registerGpuBackend(registry)` adds Impeller and its presenter to an engine's own `DeviceRegistry`. `flutter3d_app`'s `platformDevices()` does it for this platform.

### `flutter3d_lab`

- <a id="4b-lab-library"></a>**`package:flutter3d_lab/flutter3d_lab.dart`** (`migrate`). `package:flutter3d_lab/flutter3d_lab.dart` is `package:flutter3d_education/lab.dart`. `flutter3d_lab` is the `lab.dart` library of `flutter3d_education`, with the API it had; `package:flutter3d_education/flutter3d_education.dart` exports it beside its siblings. It was on pub.dev as `flutter3d_lab`.
- <a id="4b-lab-src"></a>**`package:flutter3d_lab/src/`** (`migrate`). `package:flutter3d_lab/src/` is `package:flutter3d_education/src/lab/`. What was under `flutter3d_lab`'s `lib/src/` is under `lib/src/lab/` of `flutter3d_education`. Importing it was never the public API; the main library above is.
- <a id="lab-PendulumLabRun"></a>**`PendulumLabRun`** (nothing to do). The default `gravity` is written as `standardGravity`, which is the same 9.81 m/s²; every call means what it meant.
- <a id="lab-PendulumSimulation"></a>**`PendulumSimulation`** (nothing to do). The default `gravity` is written as `standardGravity`, which is the same 9.81 m/s²; every call means what it meant.

### `flutter3d_lti`

- <a id="4b-lti-library"></a>**`package:flutter3d_lti/flutter3d_lti.dart`** (`migrate`). `package:flutter3d_lti/flutter3d_lti.dart` is `package:flutter3d_education/lti.dart`. `flutter3d_lti` is the `lti.dart` library of `flutter3d_education`, with the API it had; `package:flutter3d_education/flutter3d_education.dart` exports it beside its siblings. It was on pub.dev as `flutter3d_lti`.

What was under `flutter3d_lti`'s `lib/src/` is under `lib/src/lti/` of `flutter3d_education`. Importing it was never the public API; the main library above is.
- <a id="4a-lti-AgsClient-platform"></a>**`AgsClient.platform`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="lti-AgsException"></a>**`AgsException`** (nothing to do). `AgsException` is still a final class with the same members; it now extends `ResourceException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly. Every `on AgsException` and `on Exception` still catches it, and nothing outside the package could extend it.
- <a id="lti-LtiLaunchException"></a>**`LtiLaunchException`** (nothing to do). `LtiLaunchException` is still sealed with the same subtypes and members; it now extends `ResourceException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly. Every `on LtiLaunchException`, `on Exception` and `switch` over its subtypes still matches, and nothing outside the package could extend it.
- <a id="4a-lti-LtiLaunchValidator-platform"></a>**`LtiLaunchValidator.platform`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-lti-LtiPlatformConfig"></a>**`LtiPlatformConfig`** (`dart fix`). `LtiPlatformConfig` is `LtiPlatformSettings`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-lti-OidcLoginInitiation-platform"></a>**`OidcLoginInitiation.platform`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-lti-XapiClient-lrs"></a>**`XapiClient.lrs`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="lti-XapiException"></a>**`XapiException`** (nothing to do). `XapiException` is still a final class with the same members; it now extends `ResourceException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly. Every `on XapiException` and `on Exception` still catches it, and nothing outside the package could extend it.
- <a id="4a-lti-XapiLrsConfig"></a>**`XapiLrsConfig`** (`dart fix`). `XapiLrsConfig` is `XapiLrsSettings`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.

### `flutter3d_mcp_kit`

- <a id="4b-mcp_kit-library"></a>**`package:flutter3d_mcp_kit/flutter3d_mcp_kit.dart`** (`migrate`). `package:flutter3d_mcp_kit/flutter3d_mcp_kit.dart` is `package:flutter3d_mcp/kit.dart`. `flutter3d_mcp_kit` is the `kit.dart` library of `flutter3d_mcp`, with the API it had; `package:flutter3d_mcp/flutter3d_mcp.dart` exports it beside its siblings. It was on pub.dev as `flutter3d_mcp_kit`.
- <a id="4b-mcp_kit-src"></a>**`package:flutter3d_mcp_kit/src/`** (`migrate`). `package:flutter3d_mcp_kit/src/` is `package:flutter3d_mcp/src/kit/`. What was under `flutter3d_mcp_kit`'s `lib/src/` is under `lib/src/kit/` of `flutter3d_mcp`. Importing it was never the public API; the main library above is.
- <a id="4b-mcp_kit-project_tools"></a>**`package:flutter3d_mcp_kit/project_tools.dart`** (`migrate`). `package:flutter3d_mcp_kit/project_tools.dart` is `package:flutter3d_mcp/project_tools.dart`. The project server's tool registry, a library of `flutter3d_mcp` now, under the same name.
- <a id="mcp_kit-LoopbackMcpServer-token"></a>**`LoopbackMcpServer`** (nothing to do). Every signature is as it was. A request presents its token as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` only; one sent as `?token=` is answered 401, and a body over 64 MiB is answered 413.
- <a id="mcpkit-McpTools-addTool"></a>**`McpTools.addTool`** (by hand). `addTool` takes a `ToolSpec`, and a plugin adding one must list `PluginPermission.tools` in its manifest's `permissions`, or the call throws a `PluginException`. Declare the namespace's schema version with `declareSchemaVersion('1.0.0')` before adding tools.
- <a id="mcpkit-OfferedPrompt-prompt"></a>**`OfferedPrompt.prompt`** (by hand). The server builds the protocol prompt itself; read `name` and `description` from the `OfferedPrompt`.
- <a id="mcpkit-OfferedTool"></a>**`OfferedTool`** (by hand). `OfferedTool` takes a `ToolSpec`, not the MCP library's `Tool`. Build it with `ToolSpec(name:, description:, inputSchema: {...})`, or keep the library's schema builders inside your package and convert with `ToolSpec(inputSchema: ObjectSchema(...) as Map<String, Object?>)`.
- <a id="mcpkit-OfferedTool-tool"></a>**`OfferedTool.tool`** (`dart fix`). `OfferedTool.tool` is `OfferedTool.spec`. A tool is a `ToolSpec` now, flutter3d's own type with JSON-map schemas: read `spec.name`, `spec.description`, `spec.inputSchema`.
- <a id="mcpkit-ProjectToolRun"></a>**`ProjectToolRun`** (by hand). A project tool answers a `ToolResult` (`ToolResult.text('...')`, or `ToolResult(content: [ToolText(...), ToolImage(png)], structuredContent: {...})`), not a `CallToolResult`.
- <a id="mcpkit-ToolImage"></a>**`ToolImage`** (nothing to do). See `mcpkit-ToolText`: the other kind of `ToolContent`.
- <a id="mcpkit-ToolTableServer"></a>**`ToolTableServer`** (by hand). `ToolTableServer` holds the MCP server instead of extending it: its `MCPServer` members are gone (use `done`, `shutdown`, `serverInfo`), `toResult` returns a `ToolResult`, `onProjectCall` hears a `ToolResult`, and tools may be published as `area.verb` with `names:`.
- <a id="rc-mcp_kit-ToolTableServer-onCall"></a>**`ToolTableServer`** (by hand). `onCall` (and `onProjectCall`) is handed one `AnsweredCall` where it was handed four arguments, so a watcher keeps working when a call says more: `onCall: (name, arguments, answer, elapsed) => ...` is `onCall: (AnsweredCall<A> call) => ...`, reading `call.toolName`, `call.arguments`, `call.answer` and `call.elapsed`.
- <a id="mcpkit-ToolText"></a>**`ToolText`** (nothing to do). `ToolText` and `ToolImage` are the two kinds of `ToolContent` in flutter3d's own `ToolResult`, which replaces the MCP library's result type in every signature; there was no switch over flutter3d's content before, so none is missing a case.
- <a id="mcpkit-pictureResultOf"></a>**`pictureResultOf`** (nothing to do). It returns a `ToolResult` instead of a `CallToolResult`; a server's `toResult` takes it as before.
- <a id="mcpkit-refuseArguments"></a>**`refuseArguments`** (by hand). `refuseArguments` takes a `ToolSpec`; pass `offered.spec`.
- <a id="mcpkit-resultOf"></a>**`resultOf`** (nothing to do). It returns a `ToolResult` instead of a `CallToolResult`; a server's `toResult` takes it as before.

### `flutter3d_mesh`

- <a id="b1-mesh-Brush"></a>**`Brush`** (`dart fix`). `Brush` is `MeshBrush`. The sculpting brush is `MeshBrush`; `Brush` is the level's box in `flutter3d_sim`, and a file that imported both had to hide one.
- <a id="rc-mesh-CornerAttributes-color"></a>**`CornerAttributes`** (by hand). A corner's colour is a linear `LinearColor`, as a vertex colour is: `CornerAttributes(colour: Vector4(r, g, b, a))` is `CornerAttributes(color: LinearColor(r, g, b, a))`, and `EditMesh`'s `colorOf` answers one and `setColor` takes one.
- <a id="4a-mesh-CornerAttributes-CornerAttributes"></a>**`CornerAttributes.CornerAttributes`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-mesh-CornerAttributes-colour"></a>**`CornerAttributes.colour`** (`dart fix`). `CornerAttributes.colour` is `color`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-mesh-EditMesh-colourOf"></a>**`EditMesh.colourOf`** (`dart fix`). `EditMesh.colourOf` is `colorOf`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-mesh-EditMesh-makeConsistent"></a>**`EditMesh.makeConsistent`** (`dart fix`). `EditMesh.makeConsistent` is `ensureConsistent`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-mesh-EditMesh-setColour"></a>**`EditMesh.setColour`** (`dart fix`). `EditMesh.setColour` is `setColor`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-mesh-FaceTriangulator-fannedLastFace"></a>**`FaceTriangulator.fannedLastFace`** (`dart fix`). `FaceTriangulator.fannedLastFace` is `didFanLastFace`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="b1-mesh-ImportReport"></a>**`ImportReport`** (`dart fix`). `ImportReport` is `MeshImportReport`. What `importMeshData` reports about one mesh's weld is `MeshImportReport`; `ImportReport` is `flutter3d_model_core`'s, about a whole document imported into a project.
- <a id="4a-mesh-ImportReport-worthReporting"></a>**`ImportReport.worthReporting`** (`dart fix`). `ImportReport.worthReporting` is `isWorthReporting`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-mesh-JournalledFloats"></a>**`JournalledFloats`** (`dart fix`). `JournalledFloats` is `JournaledFloats`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-mesh-JournalledInts"></a>**`JournalledInts`** (`dart fix`). `JournalledInts` is `JournaledInts`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-mesh-MergeReport-worthReporting"></a>**`MergeReport.worthReporting`** (`dart fix`). `MergeReport.worthReporting` is `isWorthReporting`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-mesh-MeshAttribute"></a>**`MeshAttribute`** (nothing to do). Only a type its declaration names was renamed; that rename's own fix carries every use.
- <a id="mesh-MeshBvh"></a>**`MeshBvh`** (nothing to do). Only the float32 ray in its signature changed: `Ray` is `LocalRay` (`core-Ray-LocalRay` renames it).

A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.

- <a id="4a-mesh-OpResult-ok"></a>**`OpResult.ok`** (`dart fix`). `OpResult.ok` is `isOk`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.

### `flutter3d_model_core`

- <a id="4a-model_core-AssetAudit-baseCentre"></a>**`AssetAudit.baseCentre`** (`dart fix`). `AssetAudit.baseCentre` is `baseCenter`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-model_core-AssetAudit-clean"></a>**`AssetAudit.clean`** (`dart fix`). `AssetAudit.clean` is `isClean`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-model_core-BendJoint-angle"></a>**`BendJoint`** (by hand). `BendJoint(degrees:)` is `BendJoint(angle:)`, in radians, as every angle in the engine is (docs/CONTRACTS.md): pass `radians(d)` for `d` degrees. ### `flutter3d_model_mcp`

- <a id="4b-model_mcp-library"></a>**`package:flutter3d_model_mcp/flutter3d_model_mcp.dart`** (`migrate`). `package:flutter3d_model_mcp/flutter3d_model_mcp.dart` is `package:flutter3d_mcp/model.dart`. `flutter3d_model_mcp` is the `model.dart` library of `flutter3d_mcp`, with the API it had; `package:flutter3d_mcp/flutter3d_mcp.dart` exports it beside its siblings. It was on pub.dev as `flutter3d_model_mcp`.
- <a id="4b-model_mcp-src"></a>**`package:flutter3d_model_mcp/src/`** (`migrate`). `package:flutter3d_model_mcp/src/` is `package:flutter3d_mcp/src/model/`. What was under `flutter3d_model_mcp`'s `lib/src/` is under `lib/src/model/` of `flutter3d_mcp`. Importing it was never the public API; the main library above is.
- <a id="rc-model_mcp-ModelHttpServer-onToolCall"></a>**`ModelHttpServer.start`** (by hand). `onToolCall` is handed one `AnsweredCall<PictureAnswer>`; see `rc-mcp_kit-ToolTableServer-onCall`.
- <a id="rc-model_mcp-ModelMcpServer-onCall"></a>**`ModelMcpServer`** (by hand). `ModelMcpServer(onCall:)` is handed one `AnsweredCall<PictureAnswer>`; see `rc-mcp_kit-ToolTableServer-onCall`.
- <a id="model_mcp-ModelSession-root"></a>**`ModelSession`** (nothing to do). The constructor gained an optional `root:`. The MCP tools `save`, `export`, `import` and `journal` refuse a path outside the project around the session's file (the working directory with none).
- <a id="4a-model_mcp-ModelSession-import"></a>**`ModelSession.import`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-model_mcp-ModelSession-makeGameReady"></a>**`ModelSession.makeGameReady`** (`dart fix`). `ModelSession.makeGameReady` is `prepareForGame`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="model_mcp-modelMcpVersion"></a>**`modelMcpVersion`** (nothing to do). Only the value of the constant moved; code that reads `modelMcpVersion` gets the new one. It is a version or a layout size the engine stamps and checks itself.
- <a id="4b-model_mcp-kit-reexport"></a>**`package:flutter3d_mcp_kit/flutter3d_mcp_kit.dart`** (nothing to do). The kit's names the modeller's library re-exported are still exported by `package:flutter3d_mcp/model.dart`; they come from `kit.dart` of the same package, and the import moves with the rest.

### `flutter3d_net`

- <a id="net-LoopbackTransport"></a>**`LoopbackTransport`** (by hand). Use `LoopbackWire.pair(delaySteps:, lossRate:, seed:)`, whose delay is in steps: `(delaySeconds * stepsPerSecond).round()`. Its ends are slots nought and one.
- <a id="net-NetSession"></a>**`NetSession`** (by hand). `NetSession` is gone: the one rollback is `flutter3d_net`'s `RollbackSession`. Hand it the wire, this machine's `localSlot`, and an `applyAndStep` that reads every slot's frame by slot (`frames[0]`, `frames[1]`) where it read `local` and `remote`; `onSettled` gains the frames. Over an `EngineLoop`, use `EngineRollback`, which saves and restores through the loop's snapshots.
- <a id="net-NetTransport"></a>**`NetTransport`** (by hand). `NetTransport` is gone: every transport is a `PeerWire`, which a transport `extends`. `send` gains `{bool reliable = true}`.
- <a id="net-NetTransportWire"></a>**`NetTransportWire`** (by hand). Not needed any more: a transport is a `PeerWire` already. Pass the transport itself.
- <a id="net-WebSocketTransport"></a>**`WebSocketTransport`** (nothing to do). It extends `PeerWire` in place of implementing `NetTransport`, and `send` gained an optional `reliable`; making one, sending, listening and closing are unchanged.
- <a id="c-net-firstDifferingPath"></a>**`firstDifferingPath`** (`dart fix`). `firstDifferingPath` is imported from `package:flutter3d_sim/flutter3d_sim.dart`. `firstDifferingPath` is the simulation's, beside the snapshots it compares, and `flutter3d_net` no longer re-exports it.

### `flutter3d_net_webrtc`

- <a id="net_webrtc-WebRtcTransport"></a>**`WebRtcTransport`** (by hand). `WebRtcTransport` is a `PeerWire` since 1.0, the one transport `flame_multiplayer` and `flutter3d_net` speak, where it was a `NetTransport`. `createOffer` and `awaitOffer` take the signalling channel as a `PeerWire` too: hand them the relay's wire (`PeerWire.party` or your adapter) where you handed a `NetTransport`, and declare what you keep the transport in as a `PeerWire`.
- <a id="4a-net_webrtc-WebRtcTransport-awaitOffer"></a>**`WebRtcTransport.awaitOffer`** (`dart fix`). `WebRtcTransport.awaitOffer` is `openAnswering`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-net_webrtc-WebRtcTransport-createOffer"></a>**`WebRtcTransport.createOffer`** (`dart fix`). `WebRtcTransport.createOffer` is `openOffering`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.

### `flutter3d_particles`

- <a id="4a-particles-ConeEmitter-halfAngle"></a>**`ConeEmitter`** (by hand). `halfAngleDegrees` is `halfAngle`, in radians, as every angle in the engine is (docs/CONTRACTS.md): `halfAngle: 25.0 * math.pi / 180.0` for what was `halfAngleDegrees: 25.0`. An `.f3dfx` file keeps its `halfAngleDegrees` key; the reader converts.
- <a id="4a-particles-Emission-spent"></a>**`Emission.spent`** (`dart fix`). `Emission.spent` is `isSpent`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-particles-Particle-alive"></a>**`Particle.alive`** (`dart fix`). `Particle.alive` is `isAlive`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-particles-ParticleGlow-centre"></a>**`ParticleGlow.centre`** (`dart fix`). `ParticleGlow.centre` is `center`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-particles-ParticleGlow-located"></a>**`ParticleGlow.located`** (`dart fix`). `ParticleGlow.located` is `isLocated`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="particles-ParticleGravity"></a>**`ParticleGravity`** (by hand). `const ParticleGravity()` with no argument now falls by the world's gravity (`standardGravity` for a system without a world) instead of the fixed number 0.8 defaulted to, and `acceleration` is `double?`, null for that case. Pass 0.8's number to keep the old look as a look; read `acceleration ?? -standardGravity` where a number was read.
- <a id="4a-particles-ParticleSpin-ParticleSpin"></a>**`ParticleSpin.ParticleSpin`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-particles-ParticleSpin-randomiseStart"></a>**`ParticleSpin.randomiseStart`** (`dart fix`). `ParticleSpin.randomiseStart` is `randomizeStart`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="rc-particles-ParticleSystem-at"></a>**`ParticleSystem`** (nothing to do). `burst`, `emit` and `emitTimed` call their positional place `at` where it was `origin`, and say it is in scene space; no call names a positional parameter. A place in the world goes in through the new `burstInWorld`, `emitInWorld` and `emitTimedInWorld`.
- <a id="4a-particles-ParticleSystem-boundsInto"></a>**`ParticleSystem.boundsInto`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="particles-ParticleSystem-emitFor"></a>**`ParticleSystem.emitFor`** (by hand). `emitFor` emitted a whole frame's worth at once, so what it made depended on the frame rate. Call `emit` for a burst, or give the emitter its rate and call `advance(dt)`, which spends it across fixed sub-steps.

### `flutter3d_physics`

- <a id="physics-CharacterController-steep"></a>**`CharacterController`** (nothing to do). Signatures are unchanged; two outcomes move. A face too steep to stand on stops sideways and rising motion as a wall, where walking into it used to climb it; falling onto it still slides down. A body caught between a static collider and a moving one is pushed out by the static one, so a closing platform no longer sets it into the floor. A level tuned against the old slide may need its slopes looked at.
- <a id="4a-physics-CharacterController-1"></a>**`CharacterController.1`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-physics-CharacterController-tuning"></a>**`CharacterController.tuning`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="physics-ClothSettings"></a>**`ClothSettings`** (nothing to do). The default `gravity` is written as `standardGravity`, which is the same 9.81 m/s²; every call means what it meant.
- <a id="physics-Collider"></a>**`Collider`** (nothing to do). The constructor gained an optional `material:`, a `PhysicalMaterial` from the world's catalogue; every call that compiled means what it meant.
- <a id="physics-ColliderKind"></a>**`ColliderKind`** (by hand). `ColliderKind` is a class with constants (`static`, `kinematic`, `trigger`) and a `blocks` flag, so a later minor can add a role. The constants and `name` read as before; a `switch` over it needs a default case, and `ColliderKind.byName` replaces `values.byName`.
- <a id="physics-CollisionShape"></a>**`CollisionShape`** (by hand). `CollisionShape` gained a sixth case, `CustomShape`: a convex shape of a game's own, given by its support function. An exhaustive `switch` over the shapes adds a `CustomShape()` case (its `boundsHalfExtents` and `inertia` are the usual stand-ins).
- <a id="4a-physics-CollisionWorld-addBox"></a>**`CollisionWorld.addBox`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-physics-CollisionWorld-depenetrate"></a>**`CollisionWorld.depenetrate`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-physics-Contact-touching"></a>**`Contact.touching`** (`dart fix`). `Contact.touching` is `isTouching`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="physics-CustomShape"></a>**`CustomShape`** (by hand). A new case of the sealed `CollisionShape`: an exhaustive `switch` over the shapes adds `CustomShape()` (see `physics-CollisionShape`).
- <a id="physics-Dynamics"></a>**`Dynamics`** (by hand). `Dynamics.gravity` is the world's gravity, a getter on `world.properties` returning a fresh vector, and no longer defaults to 22 m/s²: it is `standardGravity` unless the world says otherwise. Set it on the world, `world.properties = world.properties.copyWith(gravity: g)`, or pass `Dynamics(gravity: g)`, which does the same; changing the returned vector in place does nothing. A game that relied on 22 sets its world to 22 where it stages.
- <a id="4a-physics-MovementTuning"></a>**`MovementTuning`** (`dart fix`). `MovementTuning` is `MovementSettings`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name.
- <a id="physics-MovementTuning-gravity"></a>**`MovementTuning.gravity`** (by hand). `MovementSettings.gravity` is `double?` and defaults to null, which falls by the world's gravity (`CollisionWorld.properties`) instead of a fixed 24 m/s². Set the game's world where it stages (the platformer's `platformerWorld`, the shooter's `shooterWorld` are 24), or pass `gravity:` to keep a character's own; read `CharacterController.gravity` where a number was read.
- <a id="4a-physics-RayHit-hit"></a>**`RayHit.hit`** (`dart fix`). `RayHit.hit` is `didHit`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="physics-RigidBody"></a>**`RigidBody`** (nothing to do). The constructor gained an optional `material:`, and `friction` and `restitution` became `double?` with the same defaults (0.6 and 0) when neither they nor a material say; every call that compiled means what it meant.
- <a id="4a-physics-SweepHit-hit"></a>**`SweepHit.hit`** (`dart fix`). `SweepHit.hit` is `didHit`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="physics-contactBetween"></a>**`contactBetween`** (`dart fix`). `contactBetween` is imported from `package:flutter3d_physics/flutter3d_physics.dart`.
- <a id="physics-pushOutsideObstacle"></a>**`pushOutsideObstacle`** (`dart fix`). `pushOutsideObstacle` is imported from `package:flutter3d_physics/flutter3d_physics.dart`.
- <a id="physics-pushParticleOutside"></a>**`pushParticleOutside`** (`dart fix`). `pushParticleOutside` is imported from `package:flutter3d_physics/flutter3d_physics.dart`.
- <a id="physics-readNumber"></a>**`readNumber`** (`dart fix`). `readNumber` is imported from `package:flutter3d_physics/flutter3d_physics.dart`.

### `flutter3d_plugin_runtime`

- <a id="f2-plugin_runtime-RuntimeShaders-addMaterials"></a>**`RuntimeShaders.RuntimeShaders`** (by hand). `RuntimeShaders` takes `addMaterials: renderer.renderSteps.addMaterials`, a function returning the `Registration` that takes a library out again, and no `removeMaterials`. Drop the `removeMaterials:` argument.

### `flutter3d_samples`

- <a id="4a-samples-kSamplesAsset"></a>**`kSamplesAsset`** (`dart fix`). `kSamplesAsset` is `samplesAsset`. A public constant is lowerCamelCase in 1.0, as Effective Dart asks, without the k prefix. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-samples-kSamplesPath"></a>**`kSamplesPath`** (`dart fix`). `kSamplesPath` is `samplesPath`. A public constant is lowerCamelCase in 1.0, as Effective Dart asks, without the k prefix. The value is the same under the new name.

### `flutter3d_shaders`

- <a id="internal-flutter3d_shaders"></a>**3 names were the package's own** (gone internal): they are not exported since 1.0.0-rc.1, and the import of `flutter3d_shaders` that brought them gets one diagnostic for all of them. <a id="shaders-package-flutter3d_shaders-stage_bindings-dart"></a>`package:flutter3d_shaders/stage_bindings.dart`, <a id="shaders-package-flutter3d_shaders-typed_blocks-dart"></a>`package:flutter3d_shaders/typed_blocks.dart`, <a id="shaders-package-flutter3d_shaders-uniform_blocks-dart"></a>`package:flutter3d_shaders/uniform_blocks.dart`. A material of your own binds by name through `RenderMaterial.parameters`, or through the accessors the `.f3dmat` build generates.
- <a id="4a-shaders-LayerInfoBlock-sceneColour"></a>**`LayerInfoBlock.sceneColour`** (`dart fix`). `LayerInfoBlock.sceneColour` is `sceneColor`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="shaders-kComputeShaders"></a>**`kComputeShaders`** (nothing to do). Only the value of the constant moved; code that reads `computeShaders` gets the new one. It is a version or a layout size the engine stamps and checks itself.
- <a id="4a-shaders-kComputeShaders"></a>**`kComputeShaders`** (`dart fix`). `kComputeShaders` is `computeShaders`. A public constant is lowerCamelCase in 1.0, as Effective Dart asks, without the k prefix.
- <a id="4a-shaders-kRequiredShaders"></a>**`kRequiredShaders`** (`dart fix`). `kRequiredShaders` is `requiredShaders`. A public constant is lowerCamelCase in 1.0, as Effective Dart asks, without the k prefix. The value is the same under the new name.

### `flutter3d_sim`

- <a id="sim-ActorSystem-events"></a>**`ActorSystem.events`** (by hand). `events` is the bus the actors publish `ActorHurt` and `ActorDied` onto, an `EventRegistry?`, not a buffer. A genre's simulation sets it in its `publishTo`, so a game that owns a genre does nothing. Before: `actors.events = myBuffer`, then `myBuffer.drain()`. After: `actors.events = loop.events` (with `declareSimulationEvents(loop.events)` once, which a genre plugin does for you), then subscribe: `loop.events.onStep<ActorDied>('game.died', (d) => ...)`. Stepped by hand, `actors.events = DirectBus()..onStep<ActorDied>(...)`.
- <a id="sim-ActorSystem-focusBody"></a>**`ActorSystem.focusBody`** (by hand). The setter is gone; the getter stays. Pass the body to the step instead of setting it beforehand: `step(focusBody: body)`, or `step(foci: [...])` for several.
- <a id="sim-ActorSystem-focusBody-setter"></a>**`ActorSystem.focusBody=`** (by hand). The deprecated setter is gone; see `sim-ActorSystem-focusBody`. Name the body in `step(focusBody: body)` or in `step(foci: [...])`.
- <a id="4a-sim-Automap-everythingRevealed"></a>**`Automap.everythingRevealed`** (`dart fix`). `Automap.everythingRevealed` is `isEverythingRevealed`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="sim-AxisAction"></a>**`AxisAction`** (nothing to do). `InputAction` is new in 1.0, the sealed family of the action kinds: `GameAction` is its button, and `AxisAction` is one of the two new kinds beside it. No 0.8 code has a `switch` over `InputAction` to be missing a case, and `GameAction` is unchanged for everything that names it.
- <a id="4a-sim-Brush-Brush"></a>**`Brush.Brush`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use. A level file keeps its `centre` key; only the Dart name changed.
- <a id="4a-sim-Brush-centre"></a>**`Brush.centre`** (`dart fix`). `Brush.centre` is `center`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-BrushFace"></a>**`BrushFace`** (nothing to do). Only a type its declaration names was renamed; that rename's own fix carries every use.
- <a id="f1-camera-CameraRig"></a>**`CameraRig`** (`dart fix`). `CameraRig` is imported from `package:flutter3d_camera/flutter3d_camera.dart`. `CameraRig` places a view, which no step reads, so it is `flutter3d_camera`'s now and `flutter3d_sim` no longer exports it. `migrate` adds the `flutter3d_camera` dependency; `dart fix` adds the import.
- <a id="4a-sim-CameraRig-extraFov"></a>**`CameraRig.extraFov`** (`dart fix`). `CameraRig.extraFov` is `extraFovY`. Units in names: a field of view is `fovY`, the vertical angle, in radians (docs/CONTRACTS.md), and time in the simulation is a `double` of seconds. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="sim-Damageable"></a>**`Damageable`** (`migrate`). `Damageable` is a base mixin class: `implements Damageable` becomes `with Damageable`, on a `base` class.
- <a id="sim-DataSourceTraceFormatException"></a>**`DataSourceTraceFormatException`** (nothing to do). `DataSourceTraceFormatException` is still a final class with the same members; it now extends `Flutter3dFormatException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly. Every `on DataSourceTraceFormatException` and `on Exception` still catches it, and nothing outside the package could extend it.
- <a id="sim-Demo"></a>**`Demo`** (nothing to do). Only the value of the constant moved; code that reads `Demo` gets the new one. It is a version or a layout size the engine stamps and checks itself.
- <a id="sim-DemoFormatException"></a>**`DemoFormatException`** (nothing to do). `DemoFormatException` is still a final class with the same members; it now extends `Flutter3dFormatException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly. Every `on DemoFormatException` and `on Exception` still catches it, and nothing outside the package could extend it.
- <a id="sim-DigestTraceFormatException"></a>**`DigestTraceFormatException`** (nothing to do). `DigestTraceFormatException` is still a final class with the same members; it now extends `Flutter3dFormatException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly. Every `on DigestTraceFormatException` and `on Exception` still catches it, and nothing outside the package could extend it.
- <a id="sim-DualAxisAction"></a>**`DualAxisAction`** (nothing to do). `InputAction` is new in 1.0, the sealed family of the action kinds: `GameAction` is its button, and `DualAxisAction` is one of the two new kinds beside it. No 0.8 code has a `switch` over `InputAction` to be missing a case, and `GameAction` is unchanged for everything that names it.
- <a id="sim-EcsWorld-restore-strict"></a>**`EcsWorld`** (nothing to do). `restore` refuses a component written by a newer codec with a `SnapshotFormatException` before it touches the world, and removes a resource that has a codec but is not in the save, where it used to keep the old one. A save this build wrote restores as before.
- <a id="4a-sim-EcsWorld-alive"></a>**`EcsWorld.alive`** (`dart fix`). `EcsWorld.alive` is `isAlive`. A boolean query reads as a question in 1.0: `alive(entity)` is `isAlive(entity)`, as on `SimWorld`.
- <a id="f1-sim-EcsWorld-exclude"></a>**`EcsWorld.exclude`** (by hand). `world.exclude<T>(because)` is `world.components.exclude<T>(because)`, the registry's own, which returns a `Registration`.
- <a id="sim-EcsWorld-query"></a>**`EcsWorld.query`** (by hand). `query()` is the query builder now (`world.query().having<A>() .without<B>().entities`, in index order). The type-parameter form that walked one component's entities in the order they were added is `queryOf<A>()`.
- <a id="f1-sim-EcsWorld-register"></a>**`EcsWorld.register`** (by hand). `world.register<T>('name', encode: e, decode: d)` was a second door to the world's component registry with no version. Register a codec: `world.components.register<T>(ComponentCodec<T>.of(id: 'name', encode: e, decode: (data, version) => d(data)))`, adding `version:` when the shape changes and `published: true` for what the view reads. The id is the same name, so a save written before reads back.
- <a id="f1-sim-EcsWorld-registerInPlace"></a>**`EcsWorld.registerInPlace`** (by hand). `world.registerInPlace<T>('name', encode: e, restore: r)` is `world.components.register<T>(InPlaceCodec<T>.of(id: 'name', encode: e, restore: (value, data, version) => r(value, data)))`: the same component restored into the instance already there, now with a version.
- <a id="f3-sim-EduDataSource-base"></a>**`EduDataSource`** (by hand). `EduDataSource` is an `abstract base class`. An adapter of your own that wrote `implements EduDataSource` now writes `extends EduDataSource` and is `final` or `base` itself.
- <a id="c-sim-Entity"></a>**`Entity`** (`dart fix`). `Entity` is imported from `package:flutter3d_plugin_api/flutter3d_plugin_api.dart`. `Entity` is the plugin API's, where a plugin's systems name it, and `flutter3d_sim` no longer re-exports it.
- <a id="sim-EntityDef-props"></a>**`EntityDef.reservedKeys`** (nothing to do). The set now names every top-level key of an entity row the format keeps for itself (`props`, `components`, `id` and keys held for later); properties are under `props`. A row without `props` still reads in the old shape.
- <a id="sim-FixedStep"></a>**`FixedStep`** (by hand). `FixedStep` is gone. A game steps through `EngineLoop`, whose step rate is a `WorldTiming` (`timing:`) and whose most steps a frame a `CatchUp`; `loop.frame(dt)` runs the steps owed and `loop.alpha` is the interpolation fraction `FixedStep.alpha` was.
- <a id="4a-sim-FlameFlicker"></a>**`FlameFlicker`** (nothing to do). Only a type its declaration names was renamed; that rename's own fix carries every use.
- <a id="sim-GameEvents"></a>**`GameEvents`** (by hand). The drained buffer is gone: a genre's simulation publishes each event onto the engine's bus at the moment it happens, and a game subscribes. Before: `for (final e in sim.events.drain()) { if (e is Jumped) jump(); }` in a system after the step. After, on the loop the genre plugin is installed in: `loop.events.onStep<Jumped>('game.jump', (d) => jump())`, or `onFrame` for sound and particles, or the whole step at its end: `loop.onStepEnd((s) => react(s.events.whereType<GameEvent>().toList()))`. A simulation stepped by hand (a test, a server) is handed a `DirectBus`: before, `final events = sim.events.drain()`; after, `final heard = <GameEvent>[];` `sim.publishTo(DirectBus()..onStep<GameEvent>('test', (d) => heard.add(d.event)))`. A buffer of your own, `final events = GameEvents(); events.add(e)`, becomes a bus you publish onto: `bus.publish(e)`, with the event declared once with a codec, `bus.declare<MyEvent>('mygame.myEvent', codec: MyEvent.codec)`. An event's `name` is the name it is declared under now (`platformer.landed`, `actor.hurt`), so code that printed or compared the old descriptive names compares the event's type or fields instead.
- <a id="sim-GameLoop"></a>**`GameLoop`** (by hand). `GameLoop` is gone; `EngineLoop` runs the fixed step as phases of systems. Make an `EngineLoop(input: …)` (its `timing:` and `catchUp:` default to what `GameLoop` did), move the body of `onStep` into a `LoopSystem` — a function of the `LoopContext`, whose `dt` is the step — added with `loop.addSystem(name, LoopPhase.physics, …)`, and call `loop.frame(dt)` where `gameLoop.advance(dt)` was called; `paused`, `alpha`, `playback` and `recorders` are on the new loop under the same names. The guide's "GameLoop to EngineLoop" section has the whole shape.
- <a id="sim-HeadlessGame"></a>**`HeadlessGame`** (by hand). `HeadlessGame` is an `abstract base class`: a game `extends HeadlessGame` (and is `final` or `base`) instead of implementing it. A game that also implemented `VersionedSimulation` overrides `SimulationVersion? get simulation` instead.
- <a id="sim-HeadlessRun"></a>**`HeadlessRun`** (by hand). `HeadlessRun` is an `abstract base class`: a run `extends HeadlessRun` (or `RestorableRun`). `position` is a `WorldPosition` (`vector.toWorldPosition()` from `flutter3d_foundation`), and `eye` is a `WorldPosition` getter that defaults to `position` instead of `void eye(Vector3 out)`.
- <a id="sim-HeadlessRun-eye"></a>**`HeadlessRun.eye`** (by hand). `eye` is a `WorldPosition` getter: read `run.eye` instead of passing a vector to fill.
- <a id="sim-HeadlessRun-position"></a>**`HeadlessRun.position`** (by hand). `position` is a `WorldPosition`, in double precision: subtract with `relativeTo`/`distanceTo`, or narrow it to a `Vector3` relative to an origin with `toVector3Relative(origin)`.
- <a id="4a-sim-Health-Health"></a>**`Health.Health`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-Health-armour"></a>**`Health.armour`** (`dart fix`). `Health.armour` is `armor`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-Health-armourShare"></a>**`Health.armourShare`** (`dart fix`). `Health.armourShare` is `armorShare`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="sim-Heightfield-exception"></a>**`Heightfield.fromJson`** (by hand). A malformed heightfield throws `LevelFormatException`, not `dart:core`'s `FormatException`: catch `LevelFormatException` (or `Flutter3dFormatException`).
- <a id="4a-sim-HeightfieldGeometry-build"></a>**`HeightfieldGeometry.build`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-HeightfieldTiles-build"></a>**`HeightfieldTiles.build`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-InputState-analogueValues"></a>**`InputState.analogueValues`** (`dart fix`). `InputState.analogueValues` is `analogValues`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="sim-InputTape-fromJson-throws"></a>**`InputTape.fromJson`** (by hand). A tape that cannot be read throws `InputTapeFormatException`, a `Flutter3dFormatException`, not `dart:core`'s `FormatException`. Catch `InputTapeFormatException`, or `Flutter3dFormatException` for every format the engine reads.
- <a id="4a-sim-JumpReach-JumpReach-of"></a>**`JumpReach.JumpReach.of`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-sim-KeyRing-take"></a>**`KeyRing.take`** (`dart fix`). `KeyRing.take` is `add`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="sim-level-format-3"></a>**`Level`** (nothing to do). Level documents are written at format 3: an `id` on every brush, light and entity, an entity's properties under `props`, overrides keyed by id paths, and the format envelope. Older documents still read and are upgraded on save; code that reads entity rows from `toJson()` finds properties under `props`. `Level` now extends `FormatDocument`.
- <a id="sim-Level"></a>**`Level`** (nothing to do). Only the constant's value moved, to 2 for prefabs and depth layers. `Level.fromJson` reads version 1 as before, and a level that uses neither is still written as version 1; code that reads `Level` gets the new number without a change.
- <a id="sim-Level-digestHex-content"></a>**`Level.digestHex`** (nothing to do). Nothing in code changes. The digest is now of the level's content, not of its format envelope, so a hash 0.8.5 stored differs from the one 1.0 computes for the same level: `resimulate` answers a run recorded by 0.8.5 with `ResimulationLevelChanged`, and a verifying server refuses it. Record such runs again. Format changes never move the digest again.
- <a id="sim-LevelFormatException"></a>**`LevelFormatException`** (nothing to do). `LevelFormatException` is still a final class with the same members; it now extends `Flutter3dFormatException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly. Every `on LevelFormatException` and `on Exception` still catches it, and nothing outside the package could extend it.
- <a id="rc-sim-LevelMaterial-baseColor"></a>**`LevelMaterial`** (by hand). `LevelMaterial.baseColor` is a linear `LinearColor`. The `Vector4` held the sRGB numbers a level stores, and a level still stores them (read and written through `storedBaseColor`), so a material made in code is `baseColor: LinearColor.fromSrgb(r, g, b)` of the old numbers and draws as it did.
- <a id="4a-sim-LevelMaterial-LevelMaterial"></a>**`LevelMaterial.LevelMaterial`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use. A level file keeps its `texelsPerMetre` key; only the Dart name changed.
- <a id="4a-sim-LevelMaterial-texelsPerMetre"></a>**`LevelMaterial.texelsPerMetre`** (`dart fix`). `LevelMaterial.texelsPerMetre` is `texelsPerMeter`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="sim-LevelPatchRefused"></a>**`LevelPatchRefused`** (by hand). `LevelPatchResult` has a new case, `LevelPatchRefused`: a `switch` over a `LevelPatchResult` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="sim-LevelPatched"></a>**`LevelPatched`** (by hand). `LevelPatchResult` has a new case, `LevelPatched`: a `switch` over a `LevelPatchResult` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="4a-sim-LevelSketch-room"></a>**`LevelSketch.room`** (nothing to do). Only a type or a positional parameter it names was renamed; no call names a positional parameter, and a type's rename carries its own fix.
- <a id="4a-sim-LightBehaviour"></a>**`LightBehaviour`** (`dart fix`). `LightBehaviour` is `LightBehavior`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-LightFixture-LightFixture"></a>**`LightFixture.LightFixture`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-LightFixture-behaviour"></a>**`LightFixture.behaviour`** (`dart fix`). `LightFixture.behaviour` is `behavior`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-LightFixtureKind-LightFixtureKind"></a>**`LightFixtureKind.LightFixtureKind`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-LightFixtureKind-defaultBehaviour"></a>**`LightFixtureKind.defaultBehaviour`** (`dart fix`). `LightFixtureKind.defaultBehaviour` is `defaultBehavior`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-Lightmap-Lightmap"></a>**`Lightmap.Lightmap`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="sim-Lightmap-exception"></a>**`Lightmap.fromBytes`** (by hand). An unreadable lightmap throws `LightmapFormatException`, not `dart:core`'s `FormatException`: catch it (or `Flutter3dFormatException`).
- <a id="4a-sim-Lightmap-kScale"></a>**`Lightmap.kScale`** (`dart fix`). `Lightmap.kScale` is `irradianceScale`. A public constant is lowerCamelCase in 1.0, as Effective Dart asks, without the k prefix. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-sim-Lightmap-texelsPerMetre"></a>**`Lightmap.texelsPerMetre`** (`dart fix`). `Lightmap.texelsPerMetre` is `texelsPerMeter`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-LightmapBaker-LightmapBaker"></a>**`LightmapBaker.LightmapBaker`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-LightmapBaker-texelsPerMetre"></a>**`LightmapBaker.texelsPerMetre`** (`dart fix`). `LightmapBaker.texelsPerMetre` is `texelsPerMeter`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-LightmapLayout-plan"></a>**`LightmapLayout.plan`** (`dart fix`). American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-LightmapLayout-texelCentre"></a>**`LightmapLayout.texelCentre`** (`dart fix`). `LightmapLayout.texelCentre` is `texelCenter`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-LightmapLayout-texelsPerMetre"></a>**`LightmapLayout.texelsPerMetre`** (`dart fix`). `LightmapLayout.texelsPerMetre` is `texelsPerMeter`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-Mover-reverseWhenBlocked"></a>**`Mover.reverseWhenBlocked`** (`dart fix`). `Mover.reverseWhenBlocked` is `reversesWhenBlocked`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="sim-MoverState"></a>**`MoverState`** (by hand). `MoverState` is a class with constants (`closed`, `opening`, `open`, `closing`); comparisons read as before, and a `switch` over it needs a default case.
- <a id="4a-sim-MovingPlatform-reverseWhenBlocked"></a>**`MovingPlatform.reverseWhenBlocked`** (`dart fix`). `MovingPlatform.reverseWhenBlocked` is `reversesWhenBlocked`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="4a-sim-NavGrid-centreOf"></a>**`NavGrid.centreOf`** (`dart fix`). `NavGrid.centreOf` is `centerOf`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-NavGrid-centreOfCell"></a>**`NavGrid.centreOfCell`** (`dart fix`). `NavGrid.centreOfCell` is `centerOfCell`. American spelling in identifiers, as Flutter and Dart use: the old British name is gone, and the new one means the same.
- <a id="sim-Navigation"></a>**`Navigation`** (nothing to do). `grid` became a getter over the grid the constructor takes, and the constructor still takes it as the one positional argument. Reading `grid` and building a `Navigation` read the same.
- <a id="4a-sim-Pace-behind"></a>**`Pace.behind`** (`dart fix`). `Pace.behind` is `isBehind`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="c-sim-Portable"></a>**`Portable`** (`dart fix`). `Portable` is imported from `package:flutter3d_foundation/flutter3d_foundation.dart`. `Portable`, the arithmetic every fixed step agrees on, is the foundation's, under every package a step runs through, and `flutter3d_sim` no longer re-exports it.
- <a id="4a-sim-PulseLight"></a>**`PulseLight`** (nothing to do). Only a type its declaration names was renamed; that rename's own fix carries every use.
- <a id="sim-ResimulationDiverged"></a>**`ResimulationDiverged`** (by hand). `Resimulation` has a new case, `ResimulationDiverged`: a `switch` over a `Resimulation` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="sim-ResimulationLevelChanged"></a>**`ResimulationLevelChanged`** (by hand). `Resimulation` has a new case, `ResimulationLevelChanged`: a `switch` over a `Resimulation` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="sim-ResimulationOnOtherPhysics"></a>**`ResimulationOnOtherPhysics`** (by hand). `Resimulation` has a new case, `ResimulationOnOtherPhysics`: a `switch` over a `Resimulation` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="sim-ResimulationRetraced"></a>**`ResimulationRetraced`** (by hand). `Resimulation` has a new case, `ResimulationRetraced`: a `switch` over a `Resimulation` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="sim-ResimulationStartDiffers"></a>**`ResimulationStartDiffers`** (by hand). `Resimulation` has a new case, `ResimulationStartDiffers`: a `switch` over a `Resimulation` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="4a-sim-RewindBuffer-keyframeDue"></a>**`RewindBuffer.keyframeDue`** (`dart fix`). `RewindBuffer.keyframeDue` is `isKeyframeDue`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.
- <a id="sim-Rider"></a>**`Rider`** (`migrate`). `Rider` is a base mixin class: `implements Rider` becomes `with Rider`, on a `base` class.
- <a id="4a-sim-RigTuning"></a>**`RigTuning`** (`dart fix`). `RigTuning` is imported from `package:flutter3d_camera/flutter3d_camera.dart`. Settings classes have one suffix in 1.0, Settings (a HAL type's is Descriptor), and each is final, const, with a copyWith that covers every field. The class is the same under the new name, and it is `flutter3d_camera`'s now: a camera places a view, which no step reads.
- <a id="sim-RunOutcome"></a>**`RunOutcome`** (by hand). `RunOutcome` is a class with constants (`playing`, `lost`, `won`), so a later minor can add an outcome. The constants, `name`, `values` and `isOver` read as before; a `switch` over it needs a default case, and `RunOutcome.byName` replaces `values.byName`.
- <a id="sim-SaveFound"></a>**`SaveFound`** (by hand). `SaveRead` has a new case, `SaveFound`: a `switch` over a `SaveRead` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="sim-SaveNotRead"></a>**`SaveNotRead`** (by hand). `SaveRead` has a new case, `SaveNotRead`: a `switch` over a `SaveRead` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="sim-SaveRefused"></a>**`SaveRefused`** (by hand). `SaveUpgrade` has a new case, `SaveRefused`: a `switch` over a `SaveUpgrade` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="sim-SaveUpgraded"></a>**`SaveUpgraded`** (by hand). `SaveUpgrade` has a new case, `SaveUpgraded`: a `switch` over a `SaveUpgrade` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="sim-ServiceDone"></a>**`ServiceDone`** (by hand). `ServiceAnswer` has a new case, `ServiceDone`: a `switch` over a `ServiceAnswer` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="sim-ServiceRefused"></a>**`ServiceRefused`** (by hand). `ServiceAnswer` has a new case, `ServiceRefused`: a `switch` over a `ServiceAnswer` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="4a-sim-SnapshotFields-flag"></a>**`SnapshotFields.flag`** (by hand). No positional `bool` in 1.0: `flag(key, true)` is `flag(key, orElse: true)`.
- <a id="sim-SnapshotFormatException"></a>**`SnapshotFormatException`** (nothing to do). `SnapshotFormatException` is still a final class with the same members; it now extends `Flutter3dFormatException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly. Every `on SnapshotFormatException` and `on Exception` still catches it, and nothing outside the package could extend it.
- <a id="4a-sim-SteadyLight"></a>**`SteadyLight`** (nothing to do). Only a type its declaration names was renamed; that rename's own fix carries every use.
- <a id="sim-StepEvents"></a>**`StepEvents`** (by hand). `has` and `count` on a drained list are gone with the buffer. Before: `events.has<Jumped>()`, `events.count<CollectibleTaken>()`. After, on the list of a step's events (`StepEventSummary.events`, or what a subscriber collected): `events.whereType<Jumped>().isNotEmpty`, `events.whereType<CollectibleTaken>().length`. Or subscribe to the type itself: `loop.events.onStep<Jumped>(...)`.
- <a id="f1-sim-StepSystems-add-order"></a>**`StepSystems.add`** (by hand). `StepSystems.add` orders by name, as the engine's loop does: `order:` is gone, and a system names the systems of its phase it runs `after:` or `before:`, by their `label:`. Where nothing is named, the order of adding decides, as equal orders did. A system that had a lower `order` than another says `before: ['<the other's label>']`.
- <a id="4a-sim-StepTimeTrace-seconds"></a>**`StepTimeTrace`** (by hand). Times are seconds now, as every time in the engine is (docs/CONTRACTS.md): `millis`, `meanMillis` and `worstMillis` are `seconds`, `meanSeconds` and `worstSeconds`, and `observe(step, cost)` takes seconds. Divide a millisecond figure by 1000, or multiply a value read back by 1000 to show milliseconds. A written trace keeps its `millis` key in milliseconds.
- <a id="sim-StepTimeTraceFormatException"></a>**`StepTimeTraceFormatException`** (nothing to do). `StepTimeTraceFormatException` is still a final class with the same members; it now extends `Flutter3dFormatException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly. Every `on StepTimeTraceFormatException` and `on Exception` still catches it, and nothing outside the package could extend it.
- <a id="f1-sim-SystemRegistration-order"></a>**`SystemRegistration.order`** (by hand). A registration has no number now: read `after` and `before`, the labels it is ordered against, and `sequence`, when it was added.
- <a id="sim-TapesAgree"></a>**`TapesAgree`** (by hand). `TapeBisection` has a new case, `TapesAgree`: a `switch` over a `TapeBisection` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="sim-TapesDiverge"></a>**`TapesDiverge`** (by hand). `TapeBisection` has a new case, `TapesDiverge`: a `switch` over a `TapeBisection` that names every case needs one for it, or a wildcard.
- <a id="4a-sim-TriggerVolume-takeOutcome"></a>**`TriggerVolume.takeOutcome`** (`dart fix`). `TriggerVolume.takeOutcome` is `drainOutcome`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="sim-VisibilityFormatException"></a>**`VisibilityFormatException`** (nothing to do). `VisibilityFormatException` is still a final class with the same members; it now extends `Flutter3dFormatException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly. Every `on VisibilityFormatException` and `on Exception` still catches it, and nothing outside the package could extend it.
- <a id="sim-WorldStep"></a>**`WorldStep`** (by hand). `dynamics` is a `RigidDynamics?` now, the interface the Dart and the native solver share. Declare what you read it into as `RigidDynamics?`, or cast with `as Dynamics?` for the Dart solver's own members.
- <a id="c-sim-physics-library"></a>**`package:flutter3d_physics/flutter3d_physics.dart`** (`dart fix`). Names re-exported from `package:flutter3d_physics/flutter3d_physics.dart` are imported from `package:flutter3d_physics/flutter3d_physics.dart`. `package:flutter3d_sim/flutter3d_sim.dart` no longer re-exports the physics. The collision world, the bodies, the queries, the character controller, the cloth and the fluids are imported from `package:flutter3d_physics/flutter3d_physics.dart`, and the project depends on `flutter3d_physics`. What 0.8's physics held and another package holds now is imported from there: `Portable` and the vector crossings from `flutter3d_foundation`, the world's properties, the materials and the constants from `flutter3d_matter`. A game that wants one import takes `flutter3d_game`, which re-exports `CollisionWorld`, `Collider` and `RigidDynamics`.

### `flutter3d_sim_mcp`

- <a id="sim_mcp-ReadingPredicateException"></a>**`ReadingPredicateException`** (nothing to do). `ReadingPredicateException` is still a final class with the same members; it now extends `Flutter3dFormatException` (and through it `Flutter3dException`) instead of implementing `Exception` directly. Every `on ReadingPredicateException` and `on Exception` still catches it, and nothing outside the package could extend it.
- <a id="sim_mcp-SimSession-root"></a>**`SimSession`** (nothing to do). The constructor gained an optional `root:` (the working directory by default); `open`, `writeRun`, `expect`, `verify` and `bisect` refuse a path outside it, as do the levels a run or a level names.
- <a id="c-sim_mcp-kit-library"></a>**`package:flutter3d_mcp_kit/flutter3d_mcp_kit.dart`** (`dart fix`). Names re-exported from `package:flutter3d_mcp_kit/flutter3d_mcp_kit.dart` are imported from `package:flutter3d_mcp/kit.dart`. `PictureAnswer` is the agent kit's, `package:flutter3d_mcp/kit.dart`, and `flutter3d_sim_mcp` no longer re-exports it.
- <a id="sim_mcp-renderMcpVersion"></a>**`renderMcpVersion`** (nothing to do). Only the value of the constant moved; code that reads `renderMcpVersion` gets the new one. It is a version or a layout size the engine stamps and checks itself.
- <a id="sim_mcp-simMcpVersion"></a>**`simMcpVersion`** (nothing to do). Only the value of the constant moved; code that reads `simMcpVersion` gets the new one. It is a version or a layout size the engine stamps and checks itself.

### `flutter3d_stereo`

- <a id="stereo-HeadTracker"></a>**`HeadTracker`** (by hand). `HeadTracker` is an `abstract base class`: write `extends HeadTracker` where you wrote `implements HeadTracker`, and mark the class `final`, `base` or `sealed`. `pose` is still yours to write; `start` and `stop` have bodies that do nothing, which your own override.
- <a id="f4-ui-StereoRig-fitToViewport"></a>**`StereoRig.fitToViewport`** (`dart fix`). `fitToViewport(verticalFieldOfView: a)` is `fitToViewport(fovY: a)`.
- <a id="f4-ui-StereoRig-fovY"></a>**`StereoRig.verticalFieldOfView`** (`dart fix`). `StereoRig.verticalFieldOfView` is `fovY`.
- <a id="f4-ui-StereoSurface-fovY-parameter"></a>**`StereoSurface.StereoSurface`** (`dart fix`). `StereoSurface(verticalFieldOfView: a)` is `StereoSurface(fovY: a)`, the same angle in radians, named as every field of view in the engine is.
- <a id="f4-ui-StereoSurface-fovY-new"></a>**`StereoSurface.fovY`** (nothing to do). `verticalFieldOfView` renamed (the entry above carries it); the name is new only to code implementing the widget, which nothing does.
- <a id="f4-ui-StereoSurface-new-members"></a>**`StereoSurface.onListenerMoved`** (nothing to do). New on `StereoSurface`, which is a widget to build, not a type to implement: nothing outside the package implements it.
- <a id="f4-ui-StereoSurface-fovY"></a>**`StereoSurface.verticalFieldOfView`** (`dart fix`). `StereoSurface.verticalFieldOfView` is `fovY`.
- <a id="4a-stereo-headRotationFromSensor"></a>**`headRotationFromSensor`** (by hand). `displayRotationDegrees:` is `displayRotation:`, a `double` in radians, as every angle in the engine is (docs/CONTRACTS.md): pass `rotation * math.pi / 180` for what the platform reports in degrees. `decodeSensorEvent` converts the platform's payload itself.

### `flutter3d_testing`

- <a id="testing-FramePacing"></a>**`FramePacing`** (`dart fix`). `FramePacing` is `PacingReport`. `flutter3d_core` has a `FramePacing` of its own, the renderer's pacing policy, so the replay's statistics are `PacingReport`, with the same members.
- <a id="testing-MaterialProgramStage"></a>**`MaterialProgramStage`** (`dart fix`). `MaterialProgramStage` is imported from `package:flutter3d_app/flutter3d_app.dart`.
- <a id="f1-replay-ReplaySubject-loop"></a>**`ReplaySubject`** (by hand). `ReplaySubject` lost `restore`, `step` and `save`. A subject now hands the replay its `loop` — an `EngineLoop` built on `ReplayStart.input`, with the game's genre installed and its `simulation` set to the run, and the game's own systems (an elements step, a camera follow on `onStepEnd`) added as the game adds them — and its `genre`, the `GenrePlugin` whose run the tape recorded. The replay restores the tape's start as the genre's part, steps with `loop.runSteps(1)` and digests the genre's part of `loop.capture()`, so recorded tapes keep passing. See `apps/flutter3d_demo_platformer/test/replay_test.dart`.
- <a id="rc-testing-renderFrame-clearColorSrgb"></a>**`renderFrame`** (`dart fix`). `renderFrame(clearColor: v)` is `renderFrame(clearColorSrgb: v)`, the same sRGB `Vector4` the view is cleared to.
- <a id="testing-replayPacing"></a>**`replayPacing`** (nothing to do). It returns the same statistics under their new name, `PacingReport`; `testing-FramePacing` renames a caller's `FramePacing`.

### `flutter3d_webgl`

- <a id="internal-flutter3d_webgl"></a>**11 names were the package's own** (gone internal): they are not exported since 1.0.0-rc.1, and the import of `flutter3d_webgl` that brought them gets one diagnostic for all of them. <a id="webgl-WebGlAttribute"></a>`WebGlAttribute`, <a id="webgl-WebGlBlock"></a>`WebGlBlock`, <a id="webgl-WebGlEncoder"></a>`WebGlEncoder`, <a id="webgl-WebGlLoadedShaderLibrary"></a>`WebGlLoadedShaderLibrary`, <a id="webgl-WebGlProgram"></a>`WebGlProgram`, <a id="webgl-WebGlShader"></a>`WebGlShader`, <a id="webgl-WebGlShaderLibrary"></a>`WebGlShaderLibrary`, <a id="webgl-WebGlTexture"></a>`WebGlTexture`, <a id="webgl-compileWebGlShader"></a>`compileWebGlShader`, <a id="webgl-declarationsOf"></a>`declarationsOf`, <a id="webgl-fragmentOutputNames"></a>`fragmentOutputNames`. An application opens the backend with `openWebGl` and adds it to a `DeviceRegistry` with `registerWebGlBackend`.
- <a id="webgl-WebGlDevice"></a>**`WebGlDevice`** (by hand). was the WebGL2 backend's own: its handle types (`WebGl*`), its capability gates (`webglGate*`, `webglRefuse*`) or its encoders. None of it is exported since 1.0. An application opens the backend with `openWebGl` and adds it to a `DeviceRegistry` with `registerWebGlBackend`.
- <a id="webgl-shader-compile-exception"></a>**`WebGlDevice`** (nothing to do). A stage that does not compile, or a pair that does not link, throws `ShaderCompileException` where it threw `StateError`. Nothing that compiled stops compiling; code that caught `StateError` around a pipeline catches the new type.
- <a id="4a-webgl-WebGlDevice-create"></a>**`WebGlDevice.create`** (`dart fix`). `WebGlDevice.create` is `open`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="f2-webgl-WebGlDevice-open"></a>**`WebGlDevice.open`** (nothing to do). Returns a `WebGlDevice`, not a nullable one, and `sources` is optional (the engine's shaders by default). A browser without WebGL2 throws a `DeviceUnavailableException`; a null check after the call can go.
- <a id="f2-webgl-WebGlDevice-readPixels"></a>**`WebGlDevice.readPixels`** (`dart fix`). `WebGlDevice.readPixels` is `readback`. See `GraphicsDevice.readPixels`: one readback, which converts a whole float target and throws where this answered null.
- <a id="webgl-WebGlSampler"></a>**`WebGlSampler`** (by hand). A `WebGlSampler` carries the GL texture target its GLSL type samples (`TEXTURE_2D`, `TEXTURE_CUBE_MAP`, `TEXTURE_2D_ARRAY`, `TEXTURE_3D`) as `target`, where it had `cube`. Test `target == WebGL2RenderingContext.TEXTURE_CUBE_MAP` where `cube` was read.
- <a id="c-webgl-WebGlSectionSources"></a>**`WebGlSectionSources`** (`dart fix`). `WebGlSectionSources` is imported from `package:flutter3d_shaders/translate.dart`. The `webgl` section of a loadable bundle is `flutter3d_shaders`' `translate.dart`, where a build hook with no Flutter SDK writes one, and `flutter3d_webgl` no longer re-exports it.
- <a id="c-webgl-decodeWebGlSection"></a>**`decodeWebGlSection`** (`dart fix`). `decodeWebGlSection` is imported from `package:flutter3d_shaders/translate.dart`.
- <a id="c-webgl-encodeWebGlSection"></a>**`encodeWebGlSection`** (`dart fix`). `encodeWebGlSection` is imported from `package:flutter3d_shaders/translate.dart`.
- <a id="b1-webgl-engineShaders"></a>**`engineShaders`** (`dart fix`). `engineShaders` is `webGlEngineShaders`. Each backend's shader table is named for its backend, so an application that opens WebGPU and falls back to WebGL imports both.
- <a id="webgl-ensureWebGlBackendRegistered"></a>**`ensureWebGlBackendRegistered`** (by hand). There is no process-wide registry since 1.0: `registerWebGlBackend(registry)` adds WebGL2 and its presenter to an engine's own `DeviceRegistry`. `flutter3d_app`'s `platformDevices()` does it for this platform.
- <a id="f2-webgl-openWebGl"></a>**`openWebGl`** (by hand). `WebGlDevice.open(width: w, height: h)` is the one way to open the backend: its shaders default to the engine's own, and it throws a `DeviceUnavailableException` where `openWebGl` threw a `StateError`. It is synchronous; drop the `await`. An engine that opens through a `DeviceRegistry` calls `registerWebGlBackend(registry)` instead.

<a id="webgpu-webgpuReleaseTexture"></a>`webgpuReleaseTexture`, <a id="webgpu-webgpuStencilFingerprint"></a>`webgpuStencilFingerprint`, <a id="webgpu-webgpuTexelBytes"></a>`webgpuTexelBytes`, <a id="webgpu-wgslFragmentOutputs"></a>`wgslFragmentOutputs`. An application opens the backend with `openWebGpu` and presents with `WebGpuFramePresenter`; nothing else is needed. A bundle's stages are read with `decodeWebGpuSection`, which stays, and handed to `WebGpuDevice.open`; packing a section is `flutter3d_shaders`' `compile.dart` and the build hook's work. Remove the import and the code that handed `engineComputeShaders` to the device.
- <a id="webgpu-GPURenderPassEncoder"></a>**`GPURenderPassEncoder`** (nothing to do). The drawing members moved to `GPURenderCommands`, which `GPURenderPassEncoder` implements alongside the new `GPURenderBundleEncoder`; every call on a pass encoder is the same.
- <a id="webgpu-GpuDeviceError"></a>**`GpuDeviceError`** (`dart fix`). `GpuDeviceError` is `GpuDeviceException`. `*Error` is for programmer mistakes, which a caller does not catch; this is something a correct program meets, so it is an exception and is named like one. `GpuDeviceException` has the same constructor and members and extends `ResourceException`.
- <a id="f2-webgpu-WebGpuAttribute"></a>**`WebGpuAttribute`** (by hand). See `WebGpuStage`.
- <a id="f2-webgpu-WebGpuBlock"></a>**`WebGpuBlock`** (by hand). See `WebGpuStage`.
- <a id="f2-webgpu-WebGpuBlockMember"></a>**`WebGpuBlockMember`** (by hand). See `WebGpuStage`.
- <a id="webgpu-WebGpuDevice"></a>**`WebGpuDevice`** (by hand). was the WebGPU backend's own: its JavaScript bindings (`GPU*`), the translation tables (`gpu*`), the pipeline cache, the encoder or the allocators. None of it is exported since 1.0, so a change to the browser's API is not a major release. An application opens the backend with `openWebGpu` and presents with `WebGpuFramePresenter`; nothing else is needed.
- <a id="webgpu-WebGpuDevice-beginFrame"></a>**`WebGpuDevice`** (nothing to do). `beginFrame` throws, once, what the browser refused since the last frame: `ShaderCompileException` for WGSL it would not compile, `DeviceResourceException` for anything else. Nothing is thrown once the device is lost, and a frame with nothing refused behaves as before.
- <a id="4a-webgpu-WebGpuDevice-create"></a>**`WebGpuDevice.create`** (`dart fix`). `WebGpuDevice.create` is `open`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.
- <a id="f2-webgpu-WebGpuDevice-open"></a>**`WebGpuDevice.open`** (nothing to do). Completes with a `WebGpuDevice`, not a nullable one, and `stages` is optional (the engine's own by default). A browser without WebGPU throws a `DeviceUnavailableException`; a null check after the call can go.
- <a id="f2-webgpu-WebGpuDevice-readBufferSync"></a>**`WebGpuDevice.readBufferSync`** (nothing to do). It only ever refused: WebGPU has no synchronous readback, and the device is not a `SynchronousBufferReadback`. Use `readBuffer` or `mapBuffer`.
- <a id="f2-webgpu-WebGpuDevice-readPixels"></a>**`WebGpuDevice.readPixels`** (`dart fix`). `WebGpuDevice.readPixels` is `readback`.
- <a id="f2-webgpu-WebGpuSampler"></a>**`WebGpuSampler`** (by hand). See `WebGpuStage`.
- <a id="webgpu-WebGpuTexture"></a>**`WebGpuTexture`** (by hand). `sampledViewIfMade` is gone: `sampledView` makes the view on first use and keeps it, so read that.
- <a id="webgpu-WebGpuTextureDimension"></a>**`WebGpuTextureDimension`** (by hand). `WebGpuTextureDimension` has new values (`oneDimensional`, `twoDimensionalArray`, `threeDimensional`, `cubeArray`): a `switch` over a `WebGpuTextureDimension` that names every value needs a case for each, or a wildcard.
- <a id="webgpu-WgslModuleCompiler"></a>**`WgslModuleCompiler`** (`migrate`). `WgslModuleCompiler` is a base mixin class: `implements WgslModuleCompiler` becomes `with WgslModuleCompiler`, on a `base` class.
- <a id="f2-webgpu-encodeWebGpuSection"></a>**`encodeWebGpuSection`** (by hand). See `WebGpuStage`.
- <a id="b1-webgpu-engineShaders"></a>**`engineShaders`** (`dart fix`). `engineShaders` is `webGpuEngineShaders`. Each backend's shader table is named for its backend, so an application that opens WebGPU and falls back to WebGL imports both.
- <a id="f2-webgpu-openWebGpu"></a>**`openWebGpu`** (by hand). `await WebGpuDevice.open(width: w, height: h)` is the one way to open the backend: its stages default to the engine's own, and it throws a `DeviceUnavailableException` where `openWebGpu` threw a `StateError`.

### `pad_input`

- <a id="4a-pad_input-PadSnapshot-connected"></a>**`PadSnapshot.connected`** (`dart fix`). `PadSnapshot.connected` is `isConnected`. A boolean getter reads as a question in 1.0: it starts with is, has, can, uses, supports, did or was, as Effective Dart asks. The value is the same under the new name.

### `pointer_lock`

- <a id="4a-pointer_lock-PointerLock-takeDelta"></a>**`PointerLock.takeDelta`** (`dart fix`). `PointerLock.takeDelta` is `drainDelta`. Creation and teardown verbs follow one convention in 1.0: create (synchronous), open (a device or a session), load (an asset), decode (bytes), parse (text), dispose (teardown), close (a connection or a stream), and drain for a read that empties what it reads. The behaviour is the same under the new name.

<!-- migration-table:end -->
